Those in need may purchase them at a reasonable price.

--- Page 4 ---
### May the Grace of Shiva be with us,
## The Science of Architecture, known as
# The Treatise of Mayan’s Measurements

---

## Invocation to the Divine
->Verse<-

Praising the feet of the Holy One who created the world,
Who manifests as the four Vedas and the Three Forms <note>The Trimurti: Brahma, Vishnu, and Shiva</note>,
I shall faithfully compose in the Tamil language
The treatise on Architecture as told by the righteous King Mayan.

(Meaning) I offer my prayers to the lotus feet of God, who created the universe and all its moving and non-moving beings to perform the three-fold tasks of creation, protection, and destruction, and who manifests as the Rig, Yajur, Sama, and Atharva Vedas, as well as the Three Forms of Brahma, Vishnu, and Rudra. I proceed to translate into Tamil the house-building treatise <term>Manai Sastra: the traditional science of domestic architecture</term> originally spoken by King Mayan, the first Vishwakarma <note>The divine architect of the gods</note> who appeared from the eyes of the five-faced Lord Shiva.
<margin>(1)</margin>

---

## Author’s Humility

If a stone is found within a mixture of fruit, ghee, milk, and honey,
Do the wise discard the whole? No, they remove the grit.
So too, when a poet speaks the colors of this sacred science,
Will the learned find fault, or will they embrace the essence?

(Meaning) Just as one would simply remove small stones found in a meal mixed with banana, ghee, milk, sugar candy, and sugar to enjoy the sweetness, the learned will overlook any errors in this book written by me—a man of simple intellect—and will instead embrace its superior qualities.
<margin>(2)</margin>

---

# The Treatise
## Omens when going to lay the foundation.

When one comes to fetch you to measure the ground with Mayan’s rod,
If you see a beautiful woman as you set out with a melting heart,
Know that great fortune has arrived;
Proceed with devotion and sincerity, for goodness shall follow.

(Meaning) When a craftsman is invited by someone to measure a site for a new house, if he encounters a beautiful woman <note>Literally "one with the form of Kama (Desire)"</note> while walking with him, it is a sign of excellent luck. He should proceed quickly to lay the foundation, and the construction will result in prosperity.
<margin>(1)</margin>

# The Science of Saraba

## Omens while standing on the house site.

If a lizard speaks from the right side and then moves to the left,
It brings great strength; like a parasol-bearing king,
The sons of that house will be free from defects and illness,
They shall possess great power; such is the nature of this offering.

(Meaning) When the master of the house <unclear>of high status</unclear> goes and stands upon the site, if a gecko <note>original: "kavuli" - a common house gecko, often used in divination</note> first chirps from the right and then from the left, it is beautiful. The owner of that house will be beloved by kings and will live happily and courageously with his sons. — Thus it is said. <sig>(4)</sig>

---

If a white pigeon, a shining cow, and likewise
A white bull are seen in their full form,
That house will be of the highest excellence and beauty,
The dwellers of that house on this earth will live like the gods.

(Meaning) If a white pigeon, a white cow, and a white bull are seen while you are standing on the house site, it is highly auspicious. — Thus it is said. <sig>(5)</sig>

---

If ants break their formation and scatter, do not proceed;
If termites are seen spreading, do not consider it;
If bees are seen burrowing, do not attempt to build;
O woman of sweet speech, these are the signs shown.

(Meaning) When the two aforementioned people <note>likely referring to the architect and the patron</note> stand on the site—if ants scatter instead of moving in a line, if termites are spread about, or if bees are seen burrowing or swarming—the house construction will not be successful. — Thus it is said. <sig>(6)</sig>

---

If the *Parandai* bird <note>The Brown Fish Owl or a similar nocturnal bird used in augury</note> sings melodiously from the right,
And then flies from the right to the left,
The house will rise high, and bright benefits will follow;
No harm shall come; it will happen exactly as described.

(Meaning) When the two aforementioned people stand on the site, if the *Parandai* bird sings on the right and then moves from the right to the left, all sorrows for the master of the house will vanish and goodness will prevail. — Thus it is said. <sig>(7)</sig>

---

A drunkard, a fisherman, an ascetic, a cripple, a dynamic oil-presser,
A man with a mutilated nose, a hunchback, or a blind man—if these appear in front,
The success of the house will vanish; do not even consider it,
O woman with beautiful hair, this is the way it is classified.

-> Its Meaning <-

(Meaning) If a drunkard, a fisherman, a cemetery ascetic <note>original: "sudukattu-andi" - a mendicant who frequents cremation grounds</note>, a cripple, an oil-presser, a person with a nose disease, a hunchback, or a blind person are seen approaching from the front, the prosperity of the house construction will be destroyed. — Thus it is said. <sig>(8)</sig>

---

A pot of liquor, flesh, a pot of water,
A full pot, a neck ornament, a young boy...
<meta>Text cuts off at bottom of page</meta>

<meta>This page continues the discourse on Manayadi Sastram, focusing on auspicious omens, the ideal slope of land, and the specific orientations and soil types assigned to the four social classes.</meta>

# Science of House Building
<note>original: "Manayadi Sastram" - a traditional Hindu architectural treatise similar to Vastu Shastra</note>

> Consider the elephant, the chariot, the human, and the horse
> If these appear before you, the undertaking will surely succeed.

(Meaning) Seeing a pot of honey, meat, milk, ghee, water, a fine garment, a married woman <note>specifically a 'Sumangali' or auspicious woman</note>, a full water pot, an elephant, a chariot, a king, or a horse approaching you from the front is considered an excellent omen. (4)

## Characteristics of High and Low Ground

> Let the directions of the Sun and Moon be beautifully low
> While the West and South are known to be high; such land is superior.
> For a life of daily prosperity without suffering, build your house there;
> This is the principle declared by all the learned poets.

(Meaning) The direction of the Sun refers to the East, and the Moon refers to the North. If these two directions are low-lying, while the South and the West are elevated, the land will yield great purity and wealth. (10)

## House Orientations for the Four Classes

-> Another Meter <-

> For the Priests, the Southern direction; for Kings, the West;
> For the Merchants, the North is the coming direction;
> For the Laborers, the Eastern direction appears;
> The center remains the place of Brahma.

(Meaning) If Brahmins <note>the priestly class</note> live facing South, Merchants <note>Vaishyas</note> facing North, and Shudras <note>the laboring class</note> facing East, they will live with the grace of Lakshmi <note>Goddess of Wealth</note>. The center should be kept as the place of the Creator. (11)

## Characteristics of the Soil

-> Another Meter <-

> Sweet-tasting earth is for the great Vedic scholars;
> Pungent earth is for the Kings;
> Pure sour-tasting earth is for the Merchants;
> Bitter earth is for the Laborers.

(Meaning) Sweet soil is suited for the Vedic scholars; pungent <note>spicy or sharp</note> soil is for Kings; sour soil is for Merchants; and bitter soil is for the Laborers. (12)

## Characteristics of Residential Houses for the Four Classes

> A sturdy South-facing house for the great Vedic scholars;
> A West-facing house for the Kings;
> A great North-facing house for the Merchants;
> An East-facing house for the Laborers.

---

<insert>* Note: "Vasi" should be understood as "Vaishya" <note>the merchant class</note>.</insert>

# The Science of Sculpture and Architecture
<note>original: "Shilpa Shastra"</note>

(Meaning) A house with a southern orientation and a north-facing entrance is for the priestly class <note>Brahmins</note>. A house with a western orientation and an east-facing entrance is for the ruling class <note>Kshatriyas</note>. A house with a northern orientation and an east-facing entrance is for the merchant class <note>Vaishyas</note>. A house with an eastern orientation and a west-facing entrance is for the laboring class <note>Shudras</note>. Thus it is said. <meta>(13)</meta>

## Rules for the Residence of Oneself and One's Elders.

### Verse

> For one's own clan and those who are superior,
> Within the dwelling of the master,
> In the directions of South and West,
> Place those of golden lineage and the rest upon the earth.

(Meaning) One should not reside in the South or West relative to those greater than oneself, such as teachers <term>Gurus: spiritual preceptors</term>, parents, relatives, or elders. It is best to place them in the South and West sections of the property. Thus it is said. <meta>(14)</meta>

> To the South, let the refuse heap shine,
> In the Southwest, place the haystack,
> Within the Western direction, build the buffalo stall,
> In the celebrated Northwest, place the grain granary,
> And in the North, construct the cow stall.

(Meaning) It is excellent to dump refuse to the South of the lived-in house, to stack haystacks in the Southwest corner, to build the buffalo stall in the West, to set up the grain storage <term>Tompai: a traditional cylindrical granary</term> in the Northwest, and to build the cow stall in the North. Thus it is said. <meta>(15)</meta>

## Further Characteristics for Constructing a Compound.

> In the Northeast direction, establish the kitchen,
> In the East, spread the stalls for the goats,
> In the Southeast, storing wealth brings prosperity,
> In the Southwest, place the heavy weights.

(Meaning) It is excellent to build the kitchen in the Northeast and the goat shed in the East. Storing valuables in the Southeast is auspicious. In the East-South <note>Southeast</note>, perform maidenly rituals; in the Fire corner <note>Southeast</note>, place the cooking area; in the South, the sleeping quarters; in the Southwest corner, the place for keeping all scientific treatises and books; in the West, the dining area; in the Wind corner <note>Northwest</note>, the cow stall; in the North, the storage for wealth and grain; and in the Northeast corner, the worship room. Doing so will result in "Royal Yoga" <note>a state of great prosperity and success</note>. Thus it is said. <meta>(16)</meta>

## Sanatkumara Vastu Rules for Commencing House Construction

> If malefic planets stand in the Ascendant, the twelfth, or the second house,
> Or if a malefic joins the crucial seventh house,
> Commencing a house under these planetary positions
> Will result in sorrow, poverty, theft, and even death. <meta>(17)</meta>

---

--- Page 8 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for traditional architecture (Vastu Shastra), focusing on the astrological alignment of the house with the owner's zodiac sign and the selection of auspicious months for construction.</meta>

If the zodiac sign of the house is favorable to the ascendant <note>Lagna: the specific zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at a particular time</note>, great prosperity will follow. However, if the house is positioned to the left, or if the Moon and Sun are under the influence of malefic planets, the life of the inhabitants will be filled with distress and deformity. <note>Verse 18</note>

### Zodiac Compatibility for House Construction

For all those who intend to build a good house, if they first align the house's zodiac sign to the front or the right of their own birth sign, their efforts will be successful. They will enjoy a long life and the blessing of good children. If the house is positioned to the rear or the left, it will result in chronic illness and death.

(Meaning) Regarding the placement of the entrance for a house: it is best to have it positioned in front of or to the right of the owner's birth sign. If it is positioned behind or to the left, it is considered evil. <note>Verse 19</note>

### Inauspicious Days for House Construction

When building a house, if the zodiac sign of the house is to the front or the right, the Goddess of Fortune <note>Lakshmi</note> will reside there. If it is to the rear or the left, even if the house is built with gold and great wealth, the very life of the inhabitants will depart. <note>Verse 20</note>

### Auspicious Months for House Construction

Chithirai, Vaikasi, Adi, the great Avani, the established Aippasi, along with the enduring Karthigai, and finally Thai and Masi—these eight months are the ones praised for beginning the construction of a new house; they bring goodness.

(Meaning) Chithirai, Vaikasi, Adi, Avani, Aippasi, Karthigai, Thai, and Masi—these eight months <note>roughly mid-April to mid-December and mid-January to mid-March</note> are good for starting house construction. <note>Verse 21</note>

### Inauspicious Months for Construction and Moving In

Panguni, Aani, Purattasi, and Margazhi—these four months are deficient. If one ignores this and builds or moves into a house during these times, it will lead to degradation, suffering, and disease.

(Meaning) Panguni, Aani, Purattasi, and Margazhi—these four months are not suitable for either starting construction or for a housewarming ceremony. If one acts contrary to this and moves in or builds, illness will arise. <note>Verse 22</note>

### Setting the Roof Ridge

Just as one establishes a Lingam <note>a devotional symbol of Shiva</note>, one must mark three lines. Draw a line for the center and the sides; the top shall be the day for the roof ridge. If the roof is set on a day that aligns with the temple tower or the mansion's structure, the house will be radiant; otherwise, the prosperity of the house will vanish.

(Meaning) Like a Lingam, draw three long curved lines. These markings will appear... [text continues to describe the base line].

# The Science of House-Building

Starting from the first position and counting from the star Ashwini: the four stars starting from Ashwini are "Ascending Days." The four starting from Mrigashirsha <note>original: "மிருகசிரம்"</note> and the four starting from Ardra <note>original: "திருவாதிரை"</note> are "Descending Days." The four starting from Magha are Ascending Days. The four starting from Chitra and the four starting from Swati <note>original: "சோதி"</note> are Descending Days. The four starting from Mula are Ascending Days. The four starting from that point and the four starting from Shatabhisha <note>original: "சதப"</note> are Descending Days. Know this well. In the Ascending Days, one may build houses, mansions, halls, and temple towers. On the peak days, the roof-ridge may be set. One must not build on Descending Days; if done, the structure will collapse and fall into ruin. <margin>(22)</margin>

---

->Other inauspicious days when the birth star and the day of the week coincide.<-

If Bharani coincides with the day of the Red-Rayed One (Sunday);
Chitra with the Prosperous Moon (Monday); Uttarashada with Mars;
Dhanishta <note>original: "அவிட்டம்"</note> with Rising Mercury; Jyeshtha <note>original: "கேட்டை"</note> with the Golden One (Jupiter);
Purvashada with Venus; and Swati with Saturn;
If a marriage is performed during these hostile alignments, she will become a widow;
If a house is built, it will become a ruin and a void;
O one with eyes like beautiful carp! If a woman reaches puberty then, she will be barren;
If a small child is born then, its intelligence will surely be consumed.

(Meaning): Sunday with Bharani, Monday with Chitra, Tuesday with Uttarashada, Wednesday with Dhanishta, Thursday with Jyeshtha, Friday with Purvashada, and Saturday with Revati <note>Note: The verse mentions Swati for Saturn, but the prose explanation clarifies Revati</note>—these combinations are not good. If a marriage is performed on these days, the woman will become a widow. If a house-foundation is laid, it will go to ruin. If a journey is undertaken, death will occur. If a girl reaches puberty <note>term: ருது (Ritu) - the first menstruation</note>, she will be barren. If a child is born, it will die. <margin>(24)</margin>

---

->Daily Compatibility and Auspicious Timing (Muhurtha) - Continued.<-

The second, fourth, sixth, eighth, and ninth days from the birth star
Are of great excellence; the others bring ruin, wealth-loss, or punishment.
The learned texts say the first, third, fifth, and seventh are weak;
All others mentioned as great will surely be compatible.

(Meaning): From the birth star (Janma Nakshatra), the 2nd, 4th, 6th, 8th, and 9th days are good. The 1st, 3rd, 5th, and 7th days are not compatible. Others are compatible. In this manner, divide the 27 stars into groups of nine. <margin>(25)</margin>

When the moon or sun is surrounded by a halo, or during harsh weeks,
During storms, rains, or when an eclipse strikes;
On bad days, during the "Singing of Venus" <note>term: வெள்ளிபாடு (Vellipadu) - a specific inauspicious planetary position</note>, or during poisonous moments;
A... <note>Text cuts off</note>

(Meaning): When a halo surrounds the Moon or Sun, during harsh weeks, overcast skies, drizzling rain, the touch of an eclipse, bad stars, the Karana called Padhi, the "Singing of Venus," poisonous time-segments (Visha Kadigai), the eighth day of the lunar phase (Ashtami), the New Moon (Amavasya), or the "Riddhai" days—avoid all these as they are not good. Choose an auspicious time (Muhurtha) on good days. <margin>(26)</margin>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for traditional Tamil architecture and astrology, specifically focusing on inauspicious days and the positioning of planetary influences.</meta>

# Science of House Building
<note>original: "மனை யடி சாத்திரம்" (Manai Adi Sastram) — A traditional treatise on Vastu Shastra and architectural astrology.</note>

### Days Without Heads, Bodies, or Legs

> If one builds a house on days without legs, bodies, or heads,
> The temple of the home will fall into ruin and desolation.
> Even if women with dark, cloud-like hair reach puberty then, it is ill-fated;
> Even if the people of the world set out on a journey, they shall find no goodness.

(Meaning): The "Legless Days" are Karthigai, Uthiram, and Uthiradam (3). The "Bodyless Days" are Mirugashirisham, Chithirai, and Avittam (3). The "Headless Days" are Punarpoosam, Visakam, and Poorattathi (3). Building a house on these nine days leads to ruin. A woman who matures <note>reaches menarche</note> during these times will be barren. Those who travel on these days will suffer distress.

### The Position of Venus (Sukran)

> If Venus is in front, the ancient lineage will face death and ruin;
> If Venus is to the right eye, known wealth will be lost;
> If Venus is behind, one will live with great happiness;
> If Venus is to the left eye, there is no sorrow at all.

<margin>(28)</margin> (Meaning): It is not good for a house or for entering a new home if the planet Venus <note>Sukran</note> is positioned directly in front or to the "right eye" <note>a specific astrological calculation of the planet's bearing</note>. If Venus is behind or to the "left eye," it is considered highly auspicious.

### Understanding the Yogini

> From the first day of the lunar cycle to the eighth, the Yogini moves;
> On the ninth she is in the sky, on the tenth she is on the earth;
> From the eleventh to the fifth of the next phase, she follows the path;
> From the sixth she returns as before;
> Thus across the thirty days of the lunar month, understand her place;
> Listen to what is good for worldly journeys;
> When the rays of Venus are not facing you, it is good.

(Meaning): Starting from the East and moving clockwise through the eight directions, the Yogini <note>a directional energy or deity</note> resides from the first day (Prathamai) to the eighth day (Attami) of the waxing moon. Following this, on the ninth day (Navami) she is in the sky, and on the tenth (Dasami) she is on the earth. From the eleventh day of the waxing phase to the thirteenth day of the waning phase, she resides as before. From the sixth day of the waning phase to the new moon, she follows the same pattern. Thus, across the thirty days of the lunar month, the dates when she resides in the Southeast, the sky, or the earth are forbidden for travel and other auspicious acts. Other directions, as well as having Venus to the right or behind, are very beneficial.

### Citation — Where the Yogini Resides

Indra, Agni, Yama, Nirruthi, Varuna, Vayu, Kubera, Isanam,
Along with the Sky and Earth, these ten in order from the first day...
<note>This verse lists the guardians of the directions to explain the Yogini's daily rotation.</note>

<summary>This page details inauspicious days categorized as "headless," "bodyless," or "legless," and provides astrological rules for the positioning of Venus and the Yogini deity during house construction and travel.</summary>

<page-num>10</page-num>
<header>The Science of Sculpture (Shilpa Shastra)</header>

->Following this method, on the day of the New Moon (Amavasya)<-
->The Yogini resides; if she is in front or to the left, it is ruinous; to the right or behind is auspicious. (30)<-

(Meaning:) From the first day of the waxing moon <note>Pūrva-pakṣa: the bright fortnight</note>: on the 1st day she is in the East; 2nd in the North; 3rd in the South-East (Agni); 4th in the South-West (Niruti); 5th in the South; 6th in the West; 7th in the North-West (Vayu); 8th in the North-East (Ishanya); 9th in the Sky (Akasha); 10th in the Underworld (Patala). Then, on the 11th day she returns to the East; 12th North; 13th South-East; 14th South-West; 15th (Full Moon) South. 

Then, during the waning moon <note>Amara-pakṣa: the dark fortnight</note>: 1st day in the West; 2nd North-West; 3rd North-East; 4th Sky; 5th Underworld. Then, 6th East; 7th North; 8th South-East; 9th South-West; 10th South; 11th West; 12th North-West; 13th North-East; 14th Sky; 15th (New Moon) Underworld. 

This Yogini <note>A directional energy or deity whose location shifts daily</note> standing in front or to the left of a person is bad for any endeavor; if she is behind or to the right, it is considered good. This is the meaning.

### For Performing Auspicious Deeds in Three Ways
#### To know the specific stars (Nakshatras).

**First:** Rohini, Ardra, Pushya, Uttara Phalguni, Hasta, Shravana, Dhanishta, and Shatabhisha. These are auspicious "Upward-facing" <term>Urdhva-mukha: stars favorable for things that grow tall or rise</term> stars. In these, it is beneficial to perform the planting of pillars <note>Stambha-pratishtha</note>, coronations, entering a new job, visiting royalty, starting a business, installing jewelry, placing the main beams or pillars for upper floors, raising flags, and planting groves or halls. Doing these auspicious deeds will lead to great prosperity.

**Second:** Ashwini, Mrigashirsha, Punarvasu, Hasta, Chitra, Swati, Anuradha, Jyeshtha, and Revati. These are "Forward-facing" <term>Tiryang-mukha: stars favorable for horizontal movement or expansion</term> stars. In these, it is excellent to buy land for cultivation, purchase cows, oxen, buffaloes, horses, elephants, or goods, start plowing with the golden plow, sow seeds, build sheds for cattle, go on journeys, dig lakes or canals, and build embankments for fields.

**Third:** Bharani, Krittika, Ashlesha, Magha, Purva Phalguni, Vishakha, Mula, Purva Ashadha, and Purva Bhadrapada. These are "Downward-facing" <term>Adho-mukha: stars favorable for things going into the ground</term> stars. In these, it is excellent to bury treasures, lay foundations, dig wells, ponds, or moats, dig pits for doorways, hide wealth, plant root vegetables, and perform the ritual "conch-installation" <note>Shanku-sthapana: a foundational ritual for a new building</note>. Among these, the first watch <note>Yama: a 3-hour period</note> is watery, the second watch is joyful, and the third watch is inferior. The twilight and nighttime should be avoided.

<summary>This page details the daily directional movement of the Yogini deity throughout the lunar month and categorizes the 27 Nakshatras into three types—Upward, Forward, and Downward-facing—to determine the best timing for specific construction and agricultural tasks.</summary>

# Important Auspicious Stars for Beginning House Construction
## Transcribed from Ancient Texts

The stars Mrigashirsha, Hasta, Pushya, Chitra, Anuradha, Uttara Phalguni, Mula, Shravana, Dhanishta, and Shatabhisha—these eleven are auspicious for the commencement of work. <note>original: "கிரகாரம்பம்" (Griharambam) - the ceremony of starting a house construction.</note>

On days that are considered evil, or when the ruling planets are unfavorable, or during the time of Venus's descent, or during the period of Rahu <note>The "Rahu Kalam" is a specific window of time each day considered inauspicious for new beginnings.</note>, even if everyone says it is a good time to rise and act, those with true wisdom and strength will not begin the framing of a house. (31)

Measuring the beauty of the gait by three-quarters of a measure <note>original: "நாழி" (Nazhi) - a traditional unit of time or volume; here likely referring to the proportional measurements of the site.</note>, and the nourishment by three-quarters, the canopy by three-quarters, and the calculated human proportions by three-quarters; as the sacred ash spreads upon the cloth, and the fragrance flourishes by three-quarters—measure these precisely as Sage Agastya <note>A legendary Vedic seer often credited with foundational texts on architecture and medicine.</note> has commanded. (32)

## The Reclining Position of the Vastu Purusha

In each month, he stretches his legs toward the specific zodiac sign; listen, O maiden, as I describe how the Vastu Purusha lies upon the earth to illuminate it, with his head resting in the opposite direction, and his right hand positioned upward toward the east. (33)

(Meaning:) The Vastu Purusha stretches his legs toward the sign of the current month, places his head in the seventh sign opposite to it, and lies with his left hand toward the east and his right hand facing upward. (34)

## Citation

In whichever direction the month's zodiac sign resides, he stretches his legs there; in the seventh sign from that, he places his head. With the left hand tucked under and the right hand placed above, within the gaze of his sacred eyes, lies the benefit of the Vastu Purusha. (35)

## The Entrance

O woman wearing heavy earrings, listen as I speak of the timing for the entrance. If the entrance is placed where there is an obstruction <note>original: "வேதை" (Vedhai) - an astrological affliction or blockage.</note>, it is said to be ruinous. If the entrance is placed facing an old temple or the sharp corner of a temple, it is ill-omened. But if the entrance is placed according to the proper hunt <note>likely a metaphor for seeking the correct alignment</note>, wealth and prosperity will surely follow.

(Meaning:) If the entrance is placed near the hand of the Vastu Purusha, it is bad for the ruler of the house. If the entrance is placed at the head, death will occur. If the entrance is placed at the side, there will be fear of the government/king. If the entrance is placed at the belly, there will be a life of prosperity. (36)

<unclear>The gait of the sun... the kings will not rise...</unclear>
<unclear>Protecting... 38</unclear>
<unclear>The Vastu Nadi time... the main entrance...</unclear> (37)

# The Science of Architecture
### (Shilpa Shastra)

(Meaning) The <term>Vastu Purusha</term><note>The "Spirit of the Building," a cosmic deity believed to reside in the earth, whose position dictates the success of construction.</note> wakes up on the following dates: the 10th of the month of Chithirai (April-May), the 21st of Vaikasi (May-June), the 11th of Adi (July-August), the 9th of Avani (August-September), the 8th of Karthigai (November-December), the 12th of Thai (January-February), and the 22nd of Masi (February-March). On these days, one may begin building a house or temple, install the main door, or perform a housewarming ceremony on dates that are personally auspicious.

->Citation: From the Master of the House<-

> He lies in Aries for ten, in the rising Taurus for twenty-one,
> In the great Cancer for eleven, in Leo for six, in Scorpio for eight,
> In Aquarius for twenty, and in the stated Capricorn for twelve;
> The wise ones of the directions say one may begin building in these places.
<note>(97)</note>

The hours, dates, and feeding times when the Vastu Purusha wakes up:
The pots of the water-bearer are eight, the stinging scorpion is ten,
The scales of Libra are two, the fine ram of Aries is five,
The mighty lion itself is a distinguished twenty-one;
The one who knows the Vedas spoke of how the Vastu Spirit is honored.

(Meaning) On the 10th of Chithirai at 5 <term>Nazhigai</term><note>A traditional unit of time equal to 24 minutes.</note>; on the 21st of Vaikasi at 8 Nazhigai; on the 11th of Adi and Aippasi at 2 Nazhigai; on the 9th of Avani at 21 Nazhigai; on the 8th of Karthigai at 10 Nazhigai; on the 12th of Thai at 8 Nazhigai; and on the 22nd of Masi at 8 Nazhigai. After these times, the Vastu Purusha wakes up and remains awake for 3.5 Nazhigai (approx. 90 minutes). Within that time: the 1st part (10 minutes) is for bathing; the 2nd part (13 minutes) is for worship; the 3rd part (13 minutes) is for eating; the 4th part (13 minutes) is for chewing betel leaf; and the 5th part (13 minutes) is for the beginning of his reign. After this sequence, he goes back to sleep.
<note>(98)</note>

> If one does not look closely at the sharp auspicious time,
> And fails to know the Vastu’s posture before measuring the house,
> The wealth of the owner will be lost, the work done will be in vain,
> Destruction will follow, and divine anger will pursue them.

(Meaning) If one measures the house site without knowing the auspicious day, the specific time, and the posture of the Vastu Spirit, wealth will be destroyed, the work will be wasted, and the architect and the owner will be followed by disease.
<note>(99)</note>

### Citation

> If you measure a house while the Vastu Spirit is asleep, it will go to ruin;
> Like building a house on a shifting riverbed without wisdom,
> The master who builds it will lose his status and be ruined;
> Therefore, wait for the moment he wakes, offer praise, and then measure the site.

->With a good day and auspicious time, in the center of the house site<-

### The Rules for Digging the First Soil

On a very good day, phase of the moon, and auspicious time,
If you find charcoal while digging, it signifies that disease will come.

# Science of House Foundations

If husk or cow dung is seen, it foretells a great loss of wealth.
If wood or firewood is found, the lineage of the family will suffer ruin.
(Meaning) When choosing an auspicious day and lunar phase to dig the foundation of a house, if charcoal or bones are unearthed, it signifies disease. If husk is seen, <note>original: "உமி" (umi)</note> it brings loss of property. If firewood is found, the lineage of the house owner will be destroyed. (41)

Finding bones, scorpions, or termites is a bad omen.
If beetles or hair are found, it brings destruction; even if relatives visit, it will be troublesome.
If a white skull or bones are seen, it signifies fear and ruin—
So say all the ancient, authoritative texts.
(Meaning) If any of the following are found: ants, scorpions, termites, wood, eggs, nails, hair, or a skull/bones, it will result in evil occurrences. (42)

### Citations

If a frog, skink, lizard, forest spider, or crab
Appear while digging, that house will be prosperous.
But if a monitor lizard, snake, heron, scorpion, tortoise, or centipede
Appear there, that very day the household will be destroyed.
(Meaning) If a frog, skink, lizard, spider, or crab appears, it is excellent <note>original: "உத்தமம்" (utthamam) - the highest grade of auspiciousness</note>. If a monitor lizard, snake, honeycomb, scorpion, tortoise, or centipede appears, that house is inauspicious and will be ruined.

If hidden treasure is found, great joy will follow.
If gold, silver, or copper are seen, it is very good.
If a cow’s horn, various grains, bricks,
Or desired metals are found, wealth will increase greatly.
(Meaning) If wealth or a treasure chest is found, there will be happiness. If a frog is found, immediate results follow. If a cow’s horn, various grains, bricks, or the five-metal alloy <term>Panchaloha: a traditional five-metal alloy of sacred significance, usually gold, silver, copper, iron, and lead/zinc</term> are found, immense wealth will be attained.

Gold, silver, and copper bring goodness when found together.
Furthermore, if iron, lead, or brass are found, it is mediocre.
O one with speech as sweet as sugarcane! If charcoal, stones, or bones are seen,
The master of that house will suffer, and his wife may leave.
(Meaning) If gold, silver, or copper is found, it is excellent. If iron, lead, or brass is found, it is of medium quality <note>original: "மத்திமம்" (madhimam)</note>. If granite stones or bones are found, the wife of the owner will depart, and the owner himself will waste away from disease and die.

If water and flowers are found in the pit, it is the way to profit.
To live prosperously, one should perform the ritual...
If one digs deep and places water and flowers within...

<page-num>54</page-num>
<header>The Science of Sculpture (Shilpa Shastra)</header>

If the water swirls to the right toward the rising sun, we shall prosper;
If it circles the earth to the left, ancient diseases will surely follow.
(Meaning) Dig a square pit in the center of the house site, one cubit <note>A 'muzham' or cubit is the length from the elbow to the tip of the middle finger, roughly 18 inches.</note> wide and one cubit deep. Fill that pit with water and flowers. If the flowers and water swirl clockwise (to the right), it is auspicious. If they swirl counter-clockwise (to the left), it signifies sorrow. — Verse 66

### ->Testing the pit by filling it at twilight<-

If you fill it with water and leave it, then at the rising of the sun,
If the water remains at a level of five parts, it is excellent;
If the water has dried up and the earth is cracked,
The learned say it foretells imprisonment and disease.

(Meaning) Fill the aforementioned pit with water at dusk. Come and observe it at sunrise: if the water remains at a level of three handfuls, there will be great enjoyment and prosperity. If the water has completely dried up, evil will occur. If the water has dried and the earth is cracked, it signifies suffering including imprisonment and illness. — Verse 67

### ->Filling the pit with the excavated earth<-

If the earth taken from the measured pit is replaced and it overflows,
Abundant wealth will follow; if it is level, there is no great gain;
If it sinks like a hollow in a pond, wealth will diminish;
O lady with jewelry! This is what the great sages have declared.

(Meaning) Pour the earth that was previously dug out back into the pit. If the soil is in excess (overflows), there will be profit. If it is exactly level, income and expenses will be equal. If the soil is insufficient to fill the hole, wealth will decrease and expenses will mount. — Verse 68

### ->The Foundation and the Installation of the Consecrated Conch<-

(Commentary) Divide the *Chara Vastu Purusha* <note>The 'Moving Vastu Purusha' is the personification of the site's energy, believed to shift positions throughout the year.</note> into parts. From the head, move five parts away; of the remaining three parts, set aside two parts. In the third part, it is best to begin the foundation or perform the *Sanku Sthapana* <note>The ritual installation of a consecrated conch or a central pillar/peg to mark the beginning of construction.</note>. The aforementioned *Chara Vastu* always lies still in this manner.

### ->The Method of Foundation and Conch Installation<-

In a house site, the *Vastu* lies with his head in the Northeast (*Ishana*) corner and his feet in the Southwest (*Niruti*) corner, facing downward. From the head, move 6 parts away; of the remaining 5 parts, divide into three; set aside two and in the other part, it is auspicious to lay the foundation and perform the *Sanku Sthapana*.

### ->Characteristics of the Master Architect<-

Adorned with golden ornaments, with a steady hand,
With the heart of Shiva in his mind, and free from doubt...
<note>The text cuts off here, describing the spiritual and physical requirements of a 'Shilpacharya' or Master Architect.</note>

# The Science of House-Building

The lineage is established, the mind is exceedingly pure;
Avoiding lower associations, he attains excellence through wisdom.

(Meaning) One who wears golden ornaments, a ring on the finger of valor, and a traditional hair-knot <note>original: "kudumi"</note>, possessing a divine physique, noble lineage, and a pure heart—avoiding the company of those outside the sacred craft—is a true Master Architect <term>Shilpacharya: a master of the arts and sciences of construction</term>. (49)

---

### The Same Subject

Being pure, reciting the Vedas, wearing clean garments,
Mind and speech becoming one, casting away anger,
Performing daily rituals and worship with completeness,
It is his nature to recite the scholarly texts every morning.

(Meaning) The Master Architect must remain clean, recite the sacred scriptures <note>original: "Vedas"</note>, and wear pure clothing or a sacred thread. With a clear and distinguished mind, his thoughts and words must be in harmony. He must abandon anger, complete his daily spiritual observances and divine worship without fail, and study the architectural treatises <note>original: "Shilpa Shastra"</note> in the early morning hours. (50)

---

### Measurement of the Cubit-Rod

The fine atom, the tip of a hair, fine sand, a sesame seed, and a grain of paddy;
The described pigeon pea—eight of these make a finger-width.
By counting six of these, a quarter-cubit is formed;
With four of those quarters, take the measurement of one cubit-rod.

(Meaning) Regarding the cubit-rod <term>Muzhakkol: a traditional linear unit of measurement</term>: 8 atoms <note>original: "Anuvu"</note> make 1 hair-tip; 8 hair-tips make 1 grain of fine sand; 8 grains of sand make 1 sesame seed; 8 sesame seeds make 1 grain of paddy; 8 grains of paddy make 1 pigeon pea; 8 pigeon peas make 1 finger-width <term>Viral: roughly 3/4 of an inch</term>. 6 finger-widths make 1 quarter-cubit; 4 quarter-cubits make 1 full cubit. (51)

---

### Another Variation

The 'Kidagu' cubit is for the tillers of the soil;
The prosperous 'Prajapatyam' is for the merchants;
The measured 'Danumudi' finger-length is for the kings;
The described 'Danukkiragam' is for the priests.

(Meaning) The measurement called *Kidagu*, consisting of 24 finger-widths, is the cubit designated for the Vellalars <note>the agricultural community</note>. The measurement called *Prajapatyam*, consisting of 25 finger-widths, is the cubit for the Vaishyas <note>the merchant community</note>. The measurement called *Danumudi*, consisting of 26 finger-widths, is the cubit for Kings <note>Kshatriyas</note>. The measurement called *Danukkiragam*, consisting of 27 finger-widths, is the cubit for Brahmins <note>the priestly community</note>. (52)

Know that eight pigeon peas make one finger-width;
Six of those established finger-widths make one span <note>original: "Saan"</note>.

# The Science of Architecture

Two fitting <term>spans</term><note>A 'span' (சாண் - saan) is a traditional unit of measurement roughly equal to the distance between the tip of the thumb and the tip of the little finger when the hand is fully extended.</note> make one <term>cubit</term><note>A 'cubit' (முழம் - muzham) is the distance from the elbow to the tip of the middle finger.</note>. Understand this measurement system clearly and without confusion.
(Meaning: 8 grains of barley make one <term>finger-width</term><note>விரல் - viral; approximately 3/4 of an inch.</note>; 12 finger-widths make one span; 2 spans make one cubit. Divide and understand the measurements in this manner.) <meta>verse number: (53)</meta>

The measuring rod is used for temples, towers, halls, and houses. We have explained the measurements for towers, halls, pavilions, and mansions. For all permanent structures and summits, use the finger-measure of the <term>kidagu</term><note>A specific long-form measuring rod used in ancient Tamil architecture.</note>. For towers and halls, use the six-finger cubit.
(Meaning: Permanent structures, summits, houses, pavilions, and mansions are to be measured with the finger-units of the *kidagu*. Towers and halls are measured with the six-finger cubit. In this system, twelve fingers are called a *visadhi*. Twice that—24 fingers—is known as a *kidagu*.) <meta>verse number: (54)</meta>

->Woods for the Measuring Rod, Various<-

Measuring rods made of bamboo are for the Vedic priests.
Rods made of flawless <term>Aacha</term><note>Hardwickia binata; a strong, durable timber.</note> are for the kings who receive tribute.
Rods made of <term>Vengai</term><note>Pterocarpus marsupium; East Indian Kino tree, prized for its strength.</note> belong to the farmers.
The fine <term>Teak</term> rod is declared for the merchant class.
(Meaning: Bamboo rods are for Brahmins; Aacha rods are for Kings; Teak rods are for Merchants; Vengai rods are for Farmers.) <meta>verse number: (55)</meta>

---

For the fitting <term>leveling rod</term><note>மட்டக்கோல் - mattakkol; a tool used to ensure surfaces are flat or horizontal.</note>, use Tamarind-Vengai, Teak, or Aacha. For the various types of houses, Tamarind-Vengai, Naga, and Aacha are suitable. For the esteemed merchants, Teak; for the farmers, Vengai. For the Gods, fashion the rod out of iron with proper form.
(Meaning: There are four types of wood suitable for the leveling rod: Tamarind-Vengai, Teak, and Aacha. Among these, the Tamarind-wood rod is for priests; the Aacha rod is for kings; the Teak rod is for merchants; the Vengai rod is for farmers. A rod made of solid iron is for the deities.) <meta>verse number: (56)</meta>

->Other<-

To explain the proportions of the leveling rod to you clearly: let one end be ten finger-widths wide...

The measurement for the set square <note>original: "Moolaimattam" - a tool used to ensure right angles</note> is ten fingers <note>term: "Viral" - a traditional unit of measurement based on the width of a finger</note> on one side and eight fingers on the other. The corner should be joined and curved into a square shape; ensure the finger measurements are precise.
(Meaning) When describing the size of the set square, one side is ten fingers long and the other side is eight fingers long. The middle corner should be bent to form a square. Study and adopt these finger measurements carefully.

---

### Dimensions for the Measuring Line:

From the fibers of palm, screw-pine, hemp, and bowstring hemp,
Along with the fine threads of the banyan and mountain ebony,
Twist a strong rope with great skill,
I shall now describe the thickness required in fingers.
(Meaning) Take the fibers from the Palmyra palm, Screw-pine, Hemp, Bowstring hemp, Banyan, and Mountain ebony <note>original: "Aathi" and "Ilathi"</note>. Combine these fibers to twist a rope that matches the thickness of a fingertip. (58)

If the rope is twisted with three strands,
It is suitable for priests <note>original: "Vippirar" - Brahmins</note>, celestial beings, and kings.
Ropes of two strands are for the merchants and laborers,
I shall now speak of the appropriate qualities for these.
(Meaning) A three-strand rope is for the gods, the learned priests, and the rulers. A two-strand rope is for the merchant class and the agriculturists <note>original: "Velalar"</note>. (59)

### The Installation of the Gnomon:

The length of the gnomon <note>term: "Sangu" - a conical or cylindrical peg used in ancient surveying to determine directions via shadows</note> is twelve fingers,
Its thickness is determined by the same measure,
The circumference at the top is three-fourths of that,
It should be crafted to taper perfectly to a point.
(Meaning) The length of the gnomon is twelve fingers. Its thickness is one and three-quarters <insert>at the base</insert>. The circumference at the head is derived from this, and the top should be shaped into a fine point. (60)

### Another method:

For the gods of the sky down to the humans of earth,
I shall name the woods suitable for the gnomon:
The Sacred Fig, the Banyan, the Cluster Fig, and the Sissoo,
The dark Ebony and the Indian Rosewood; carve from these.
(Meaning) For both divine and human purposes, the wood for the gnomon should be sourced from the Sacred Fig <note>Peepal</note>, Banyan, Cluster Fig, Sissoo, Ebony, or Rosewood. (61)

<page-num>28</page-num>
<header>The Science of Architecture</header>

After taking the measurements for the length and level of the ground equally,
Place the faultless conch <note>The "Sanku" or gnomon/conch is used for orientation and as a ritual foundation stone</note> using a solid material.
(Meaning:) When placing the conch, level the ground and take a measurement of one rod <term>Kole (a traditional unit of length)</term> in width and length equally. Place the material and then set the conch. <meta>(62)</meta>

# Other Rules

The months of Avani, Aippasi, and the great month of Kartigai,
Are for the long-lived ancients who dwell upon the flower-adorned earth;
Except for these, which dispel the darkness, the others are for the Gods;
In the beautiful portico, one may establish the foundation.
(Meaning:) The three months of Avani <note>mid-August to mid-September</note>, Aippasi <note>mid-October to mid-November</note>, and Kartigai <note>mid-November to mid-December</note> are the months for humans to lay the foundation. All other months are reserved for the Gods. <meta>( )</meta>

# The Casket for the Womb-Deposit

The earth raised by a crab, the earth from an elephant's tusk,
The earth from a long-standing termite mound, the earth from a deep pond,
And the earth tossed by a bull; gather all these together,
And fashion the casket to hold the powerful "womb" <term>Garbha (the ritual deposit placed under a building)</term>.
(Meaning:) Collect soil dug up by a crab, soil from an elephant’s tusk <note>soil stuck to the tusks after an elephant gores the earth</note>, soil from a termite mound, soil from a pond, and soil from a bull’s horns. Mix all these to create the "womb casket." <meta>(64)</meta>

Fashion the womb-casket with five, seven, or nine divisions,
Or even counting up to fifteen chambers;
Place within them precious jewels, gold, and good varieties of grain,
Firmly packed, as the masters of the arts have commanded.
(Meaning:) Create a casket with five, seven, nine, or fifteen chambers. In the center chamber, place gold and jewelry. In the surrounding chambers, place various types of grain.

Taking the center of the hall and then its width,
Divide it into nine parts; leave four to the West,
From the pure East leave three, from the South leave four,
And from the virtuous North leave three; in that center, place the womb.
(Meaning:) First find the center point of the hall, then the width, and divide the area into nine parts. Leave four parts on the West, three on the East, four on the South, and three on the North. Place the ritual "womb" in the remaining center. <meta>(66)</meta>

If the architect <term>Silpi (traditional master builder)</term> who knows this science performs these acts, know that it will remove the faults of the house, the faults of the workmanship, and the faults of the land.

### Measurement for Driving the Foundation Stakes

The length of the stake should be eleven fingers <note>A traditional unit of measurement based on the width of a finger</note>, or it may increase by one finger up to twenty-four fingers in total. When driving these stakes, first determine the center of the *Vastu Purusha* <note>The "spirit of the site" or cosmic man inhabiting the ground</note> at the belly, then proceed to the southern corner and continue in a clockwise direction.

(Meaning) The length of the stake should be between 11 and 24 fingers. When driving the stakes, one must first identify the belly of the *Vastu* deity, then drive the first stake in the southwest corner, followed by the southeast, northeast, and northwest corners. (67)

---

### Faults in Timber Joining and Door Placement

If a driven stake is knocked or pulled out, if the measuring rope snaps while being held, if a pillar is planted and then removed, if a single timber is joined in the middle of the house, or if a planned doorway is moved to a different location—death will surely follow such violations of the law.

(Meaning) It is a severe defect <term>Dosha: a spiritual flaw or negative influence</term> to knock or pull out a stake once driven, to snap the thick measuring rope, to uproot a pillar after it has been set, to join timber in the center of the house, or to alter the position of a doorway once it has been established. (68)

---

### Defects in the Roof Ridge and Ropes

If the roof ridge is crooked or if the ropes are crossed while building the house, it will result in terminal illness. The inhabitants will face distress, and the mistress of the house will suffer from deep-seated enmity and hardship; this is the truth.

(Meaning) Building a house where the roof ridge is misaligned or where the structural ropes are crossed will manifest as terrible defects. (69)

---

### Extensions and Upper Floors

If a house is extended toward the West or South, great faults will arise. If extended toward the North or East, it will bring attraction and prosperity. Excessive projections or slants are not good; likewise, crooked corners or misaligned upper floors are forbidden.

(Meaning) One should not extend a house toward the West or South. Extending toward the North or East is beneficial. However, having excessive projections, slants, or building upper floors with crooked, receding corners is prohibited. (70)

---

### Rules for the Sacred Thread for the Four Classes

For the Brahmin <note>The priestly class</note>, use a thread made of *Dharba* grass <note>Desmostachya bipinnata, a sacred grass used in rituals</note> with three strands. For the King <note>Kshatriya/Ruling class</note>, use a thread made of cotton with two strands. For the Merchant <note>Vaishya class</note>, use two strands made of palm fiber. For the wise Farmer <note>Shudra/Laboring class</note>, use two strands made of fiber from the *Atthi* <note>Ficus racemosa or Cluster Fig tree</note> tree.

<summary>This page details the ritual dimensions for foundation stakes, warns against structural changes that bring "Dosha" (spiritual defects), and specifies the materials for sacred threads based on social class.</summary>

For the Merchants, let it be two strands of palm fiber. For the Laborers, let it be two strands of *Aathi* <note>Bauhinia</note> fiber. — Thus it is said. (71)

# Leveling the Ground

If you wish to find the water level, consider the site carefully. Dig a firm, square pit in the center and quickly fill it with water. Firmly drive stakes into the corners and then tie the measuring cords.

(Meaning) To find the ground level, dig a square pit in the middle, pour in water, drive stakes into the four directions, and tie the cords. (72)

---

If the water poured in flows well, reaching the four directions, touching the stakes and spreading evenly, then the ground is level. The learned architects, having observed this on the temple grounds, shall offer prayers with focused minds. <note>Verse 73 is unnumbered in the original margin but follows 72.</note>

(Meaning) If the water poured in the center overflows, spreads to the four directions, and touches the stakes and the cords, it is a level surface. — Thus it is said.

# Setting the Roof Pillars and Auspicious Timing

Divide the width into nine parts; one part is for the roof pillar. Two parts shall go into the pit below. For the portion that rises above, six parts are allocated. Of those, one part goes to the top joint, and the remaining five parts shall stand in the middle for all to see.

(Meaning) Measure the width and divide it into 9 parts. One part is the thickness of the pillar. 2 parts are for the depth of the foundation pit. Of the remaining 6 parts above ground, 1 part is for the capital/joint, and 5 parts are for the standing height. — Thus it is said. (74)

# Rules for Planting the Corner Pillars

At the chosen auspicious time <term>Muhurtha<gloss>an astrologically favorable moment</gloss></term>, first perform a puja <note>ritual worship</note> to Lord Ganesha. Place gold, precious gems, and milk into the pit. With great ceremony, plant the first pillar in the Southwest corner. Then, proceed to plant the Southeast, Northeast, and Northwest corner pillars in their proper order.

(Meaning) At a good Muhurtha, worship Lord Vinayaka <note>Ganesha</note>, drop gold, fine gems, and milk into the pit, and plant the Southwest corner pillar first. Then plant the Southeast, Northeast, and Northwest corner pillars according to the rules. — Thus it is said. (75)

# Joining the Timber

If the wood is joined such that the base of the tree is at the bottom and the tip is at the top, with the sapwood on the outside and the heartwood on the inside, those who dwell there will have a life of prosperity and wealth. If this is done incorrectly, it will surely bring disease and ruin.

(Meaning) When joining timber, if the "old head" <note>the thicker, base end of the timber</note> is placed below and the "young head" <note>the thinner, top end</note> is placed above, it is a harmonious arrangement. If the old head is placed inside and the young head is placed outside, it is considered good. If this is done incorrectly, disease and ruin will occur. Place both old heads together in the Southwest corner.
When joining them permanently, let the southern head be on top.
Place both young heads in the Northeast corner.
The rule states the northern head should be placed below when binding them.

### Rules for Joining Beams After Identifying Good and Bad Wood

For storied houses, palaces, and temples,
In the long, intersecting rows of the virgin-path <note>Kanni-paththi: a specific architectural alignment</note>,

Knowing these instructions and the "Elephant's State" <note>Anaiyin-nilai: a specific structural measurement or omen</note> (80)

If you wish to join the beams, listen all you in the world:
Measure the interior of the house and divide it into a hundred distances.
Seeing the form clearly with a plumb line,
This is the good rule spoken for joining. (81)

If the joining of the beams <term>Utthiram<gloss>Main supporting cross-beam</gloss></term> is done strongly in this world,
Through that, many internal blessings will flourish.

## Grammar for Setting Pillars and Hand-Railing

If the forest timber is cut during the Moon's phase <note>Monday</note>, the hand-rail set on a strong Wednesday,
The roofing completed by destiny on a Friday, and the house-warming done on a Thursday,
Even if enemies come with conspiracies or fire surrounds the dwelling,
The inhabitants will live with the love of the golden Indra <note>King of the Gods</note>.

(Meaning): If you plant the pillar on a Monday, set the hand-rail on a Wednesday, roof the house on a Friday, and enter to reside on a Thursday, you will obtain the eight forms of wealth like Lord Indra and live for a long time with undiminishing fame. (85)

## Rules for Placing the Doorframe

Divide the measured cubits of the chosen house site
Into nine equal parts; leave three parts to the left,
And leave the remaining parts to the right; in the portion that stands between,
Place the entrance with love, as the ancients have prescribed. (86)
Measure the cubits of the flawless house;
For human beings, the entrance should be to the left of the center.
O one with tresses sweet as honey! For the Gods, the center
Is the proper place for the entrance, as known by the wise. (87)

### Another Verse

For the tongue-groove <note>the joint</note>, the three-part middle is the center;
For the pillar and the door-frame, this is the same.
For the top and the bottom, the middle is the body;
Align the palm-leaf measurements and the marking-thread accordingly.

### Distinguishing Male and Female in Timber—Another Verse

If the base and the tip are of equal thickness, they call it a "Neuter" tree.
If the base is thick, it is "Female"; if the tip is thick, it is "Male."
Use these for beautiful pillars, curves, and half-beams;
For rafters and cross-beams, any of these may be used as appropriate. (88)

# Science of House Building

Divide the width of a foot into two parts;
For all people, five parts are fitting for the appearance.
If it exceeds five and reaches six, it is called *Dhruva*.
If it reaches eight, it is *Koti*, and nine is also *Koti*. (10)
<note>original: "மனையடிசாஸ்திரம்" (Manaiyadi Sastram). This refers to the traditional Vastu science of measurements and proportions for domestic architecture.</note>

In five parts, see two as the inner measurement;
If it exceeds by three cubits, add one more.
Without fail, eight is the standard for the pillars;
If it exceeds by two, it can be classified as another type. (11)
<note>A "cubit" (muzham) is a traditional unit of measurement from the elbow to the tip of the middle finger.</note>

---

### Male Tree

When observing trees with careful intent,
If it is rounded like a pillar, tall, and grows straight,
Standing majestically upon the wide earth,
The experts of the Vedas have declared it a "Male Tree."
<gloss>(Meaning) If a tree is rounded like a pillar and grows tall and straight, it is called a Male Tree. (12)</gloss>

### Female Tree

When observing trees with careful consideration,
If the base is thick and the top tapers to be thin,
It can be classified and grandly recited as "Female."
The Tamil scholars and the wise have spoken of its nature.
<gloss>(Meaning) If a tree has a thick base and a thin tip, it is called a Female Tree. (14)</gloss>

### Neuter Tree

When observing the nature of trees,
If it is thin and long upon the ground,
And then the head (top) becomes very thick as it stands,
The experts of the Vedas have declared it a "Neuter Tree."
<note>original: "அலிமரம்" (Ali maram). In traditional botany/architecture, this refers to trees that do not follow the standard tapering or uniform patterns.</note>
<gloss>(Meaning) If a tree is thin and long but has a thick top, it is called a Neuter Tree.</gloss>

If it is considered a Male Tree, it is fit for legs and pillars;
If it is a graceful Female Tree, it is fit for main beams and corbels;
If it is a Neuter Tree, it is fit for cross-beams and rafters;
If used for strong supports, these are the best of timbers.

<gloss>(Meaning) Male trees are used for legs and pillars; Female trees are used for main beams and corbels; Neuter trees are used for cross-beams, rafters, and supports. These are the excellent ways to use timber. (15)</gloss>
<note>A "corbel" (pothigai) is a structural piece of stone or wood jutting from a wall to carry a weight, often elaborately carved in Tamil architecture.</note>

## Trees Unfit for House Construction

> The *Agathi*, the Banyan, the *Icchi*, the *Arasu* <note>Sacred Fig</note>, and the *Ilavu* <note>Silk Cotton</note>,
> The *Purasu*, the *Kuccham*, the *Ilanthai* <note>Jujube</note>, and the *Pili*,
> The wise *Magizhu*, the *Vila* <note>Wood Apple</note>, and all such leafy trees—
> If a house is built with these, wealth and beauty will surely diminish.

<gloss>(Meaning)</gloss> If one builds a house using *Agathi*, Banyan, *Icchi*, *Arasu*, *Ilavu*, *Purasu*, *Kuccham*, *Ilanthai*, *Pili*, *Magizhu*, or *Vila*, the existing wealth and the blessing of children will decrease, poverty will prevail, and great suffering will be caused. <margin>(37)</margin>

> Trees fallen in a powerful wind, or those
> Taken from a sacred temple, or those
> Standing in a cremation ground—
> If a house is built of these, the lineage will be destroyed.

If a house is constructed using trees blown down by the wind, trees belonging to a temple, or trees from a cremation ground, wealth will dwindle, the lifespan will be exhausted, and the entire lineage will face ruin. <margin>(38)</margin>

## Venpa Verse

> Cotton, *Agathi*, Palm, *Naval* <note>Jamun</note>, and *Nelli* <note>Gooseberry</note>,
> *Erukku* <note>Calotropis</note>, Tamarind, and *Ilavu*—these eight increase [misfortune];
> No matter the house, even if it be the abode of Indra,
> That house will surely fall into ruin.

<gloss>Meaning:</gloss> Trees such as Cotton, *Agathi*, Palm, *Naval*, *Nelli*, *Erukku*, Tamarind, and *Ilavu* should not be present within the house grounds. Goddess Lakshmi, who resides in the lotus of other trees, will be unwilling to stay in such a place; she will despise it, leave the house, and the home will become a wasteland.

> Whether it be three-quarters or one-quarter of the width,
> The measurement must align with the proportions derived from the land's width.

# Science of House Building <note>original: "Manadi Sastram" - a classical Tamil treatise on Vastu Shastra or architectural science</note>

<margin>(101)</margin> Those who divide the measurements into eight parts will wisely take two parts for the western opening.

Consider dividing the portion into one and a half, then further into five parts; to know the auspicious alignment for the nine <note>referring to the nine planetary or directional influences</note>, two parts shall be assigned to the soft wood sections; these three <margin>(102)</margin> shall be placed accordingly.

Divide the established wall thickness into equal parts; place the center correctly on both sides, ensuring the exterior is balanced in four parts; this is the fitting method for kings, <margin>(103)</margin> as clarified by the tradition of the song-books.

## Rules for Setting the Length of the Threshold

When setting the length as one, set it aside by one finger-width <term>virali (finger-width): a traditional unit of measurement approximately 3/4 of an inch</term>. Whatever the length, divide it into 8 parts; let the inner frame be 7 parts. Divide into 11; let the top be 5 and the bottom be 4 to keep the level even. For the threshold length, divide into 9, making the inner frame 2 parts; divide into 9 again, with the top 5 and bottom 4. For another threshold length, divide into 7, making the inner frame 6 parts; divide into 9, with the top 5 and bottom 4. One must understand these proportions to set them firmly; the inner alignment is achieved by subtracting half the inner line and adjusting by a finger-width.

## Characteristics of Doors

The closing and opening of doors for the glorious Gods and for humans must be distinct. For the Gods, two bolts are excellent; for others, a single latch. <margin>(104)</margin> The door rod should be crafted firmly and with excellence.

If made of two types of wood, use one bolt first; two bolts are for the hidden chambers of the Gods, while the rest are for common dwellings. Consider that if the thickness is doubled for the Gods, it brings grace; if the proportions are seen to be correct, it will be a great path for the people.

## Also - Characteristics of Doors (Venpa Verse)

If the door scrapes the floor or makes a loud "O" sound when moving,
It will cause the residents to wither and suffer;
But if it stands firm without swaying, the house remains strong;
If the position is lost, ruin follows.
The door must join the frame perfectly.

(Meaning): If the finished door is opened and stands still, it is well. If it swings shut on its own or makes a great noise, it does not bring high prosperity but rather ruins the house. If it stands exactly where it is placed, one will live for a very long time. <margin>(106)</margin> Thus it is said.

## Viruttam Verse

When the door stands at positions one, three, or five,
The support and the sound will be distinct and superior.

### Rules for Placing the Entrance According to the Position of the Vastu Purusha

In the three months of **Panguni, Chithirai, and Vaikasi** <note>mid-March to mid-June</note>, as the head of the Vastu Purusha <note>the cosmic man/deity of the site</note> lies to the West, the entrance should be placed on the North.

In the three months of **Aani, Aadi, and Aavani** <note>mid-June to mid-September</note>, as the head lies to the North, the entrance should be placed on the East.

In the three months of **Purattasi, Aippasi, and Karthigai** <note>mid-September to mid-December</note>, as the head lies to the East, the entrance should be placed on the South.

# The Results of the Sacred Vastu House - Citations
## Verse

If the entrance is located at the pure belly <note>the center of the site</note>, wealth and domestic harmony will flourish.
If the entrance is placed at the hand, children will suffer and the house will fall into ruin.
If it is placed at the chest, great honors and status will be lost.
If it is placed slowly toward the head, the master of the house will surely perish.

---

# The Zodiac Houses of the Vastu Purusha

When the sun is in Pisces, Aries, or Taurus, the Vastu Purusha lies down with his feet extended toward the East.
Facing the North, with his left hand below and his right hand above,
He stretches his legs toward the direction of Indra <note>East</note>.
O woman with hair as sweet as honey, listen to how the Vastu Purusha lies.

<term>Meaning</term> <note>original: "இ-ள்" - abbreviation for 'Ithalporul' meaning 'this is the meaning'</note> In the three signs of Pisces, Aries, and Taurus, he extends his feet; in Virgo, Libra, and Scorpio, he places his head. With his left hand down and right hand up, facing the North, the Vastu Purusha lies with his feet toward the direction of Indra.

When the sun is in Gemini, Cancer, or Leo, he places his head toward the North.
With his left hand toward the East and his right hand raised above,
Stretching his powerful legs toward the direction where Yama <note>the god of death; South</note> dwells.
O woman with hair like a swarm of bees, listen to how the Vastu Purusha lies.

<term>Meaning</term> In the three signs of Gemini, Cancer, and Leo, he extends his feet; in Sagittarius, Capricorn, and Aquarius, he places his head. With his left hand down and right hand up, he lies with his feet extended toward the corner of Yama.

When the sun is in Virgo, Libra, or Scorpio, he places his feet toward the direction of the sun <note>West</note>.
Stretching his legs toward the West where Varuna <note>god of the oceans</note> dwells.
With his left hand toward the East and facing the direction of Yama,
O woman of beautiful form, listen to how the Vastu Purusha lies.

<term>Meaning</term> In the three signs of Virgo, Libra, and Scorpio, he extends his feet; in Pisces, Aries, and Taurus, he places his head. With his left hand down and right hand up, facing the direction of Yama, the Vastu Purusha lies.

When the sun is in Sagittarius, Capricorn, or Aquarius, he places his head toward the South.
With his left hand toward the East and his right hand raised above,
With his mind and face toward the West, stretching his legs toward the North.
O woman of the forest, listen to how the Vastu Purusha lies.

# The Science of Sculpture

<note>original: "சிற்பசாஸ்திரம்" (Shilpa Shastra). This refers to the traditional Hindu canons of sculpture and architecture.</note>

[Meaning] In the three zodiac signs of Sagittarius, Capricorn, and Aquarius, he stretches his legs; in the three signs of Gemini, Cancer, and Leo, he places his head. With his left hand below and his right hand above, facing West and his back to the North, the <term>Vastu Purusha</term><note>The Vastu Purusha is the "spirit of the building site," envisioned as a giant lying on the earth whose body defines the sacred geometry of a structure.</note> lies down.

->The Position of Vastu<-

With the head placed excellently in one zodiac sign,
And the legs stretched in the seventh sign from there, known as Gemini,
Established in the <term>Sashaka</term><note>A specific ritual posture or measurement style</note> position,
O maiden, listen as I describe how Vastu lies.

[Meaning] Placing the head in one zodiac sign and stretching the legs in the seventh sign from it, the Vastu Purusha lies in the Sashaka establishment.

### Another Verse

Knowing the pure position where the mighty Sun stands,
Stretching the limbs across the months, with the head placed below in the sign,
With the left hand positioned underneath and the right hand placed above,
O maiden, this is how the Vastu Purusha lies upon the earth.

<margin>117</margin>
[Meaning] Placing the feet in the zodiac sign where the Sun resides and the head in the seventh sign from it, with the left hand below and the right hand above, the Vastu Purusha lies.

### Another Verse

Lying with the left hand below, he who lay there rises;
If his movement is toward the right, it is auspicious for the zodiac signs.
By observing the belly while he lies and calculating the entrance with care,
If the house is not released <insert>built</insert> accordingly, great suffering will follow, O maiden.

<margin>118</margin>
[Meaning] He who lay with the left hand below and right hand above rises and moves to the right; if one does not use the <term>Manai Kol</term><note>A traditional architect's measuring rod</note> to fix the entrance based on the position of the belly, they will come into great distress.

### Another Verse

Without knowing the fixed state of the residing Vastu,
If one begins to use the pure measuring rod for the house,
The beloved wife and the children,
And even the learned sculptor, will meet their end.

<margin>119</margin>
[Meaning] If the measuring rod is used without knowing the exact determination of Vastu, the master of the house, his wife, and the sculptor who set the foundation will perish.

If the measurement of the house is certain and prosperous,
And if one proceeds according to the ancient Vastu Purusha,
Enemies and divisions will be removed,
And sinful diseases will vanish from the body.

# Days When the Earth-Spirit Awakens

The months of Chithirai, Vaikasi, Adi, and the excellent Avani,
Along with the established Aippasi and the enduring Karthigai,
And including the months of Thai and Masi—these eight are the ones.
If one begins building a house with wisdom during these times, it shall bring fame and goodness.

(Meaning) Chithirai, Vaikasi, Adi, Avani, Aippasi, Karthigai, Thai, and Masi <note>These correspond roughly to mid-April through mid-December, and mid-January through mid-March</note>—in these eight months, the *Vastu Purusha* <note>The "Earth-Spirit" or personification of the site</note> awakens. If one lays the foundation for a house in these months, all forms of wealth will manifest. (121)

## The Results of Omens

If you see a full water pot, permanent wealth will be attained.
If you see an empty or leaking pot, it signifies that sorrow will occur.
If you see a slithering snake, unnatural enmity will arise.
If you see a blazing fire, the precious household will be scattered and lost. (122)

If a black object is seen, the body will suffer from fiery diseases.
If a proper bone is seen, all the livestock belonging to the master will perish.
If a hide or skin is seen, all great wealth will be ruined.
If burning firewood is seen, there will be finality and karmic troubles. (123)

If a pregnant woman is seen, double wealth and prosperity will occur.
If a large frog is seen, a great army <note>likely implying power or a large retinue</note> will be gained.
If a desired crab is seen, great good fortune will arise.
If seen with an earthen vessel, strength and form will fade away. (124)

## The Rule for Testing Water in the Foundation Pit

When you have dug a bright pit and poured pure water into it:
If the water swirls in a clockwise direction <note>original: "Valampuri", like the rare right-handed conch</note>, life and prosperity will follow.
If the water remains stagnant or sinks poorly, that house site is not suitable;
Even the head of the lineage will fall prey to cruel diseases. (125)

## Rules for Knowing Good and Evil Based on Stars

Regarding the width and height of the house, if one asks what the results are for a house built in the direction of the pot <note>likely referring to the Kumbha or Aquarius direction/alignment</note>:

**Ashwini Star:** For this star, the house length should be six *cubits* <term>Muzham: a traditional measurement from the elbow to the tip of the middle finger</term>, the width should be three and a quarter *cubits*. The entrance should face North. The doorframe height should be 46 inches.

**Bharani Star:** For this star, the length should be 7 *cubits*, the width 3 *cubits*. The entrance should face North. The doorframe height should be 51 inches.

For the star **Shatabhisha** <note>original: "Sathayam"</note>, the southern orientation: for a length of 1 cubit <note>term: "Muzham" - a traditional unit of measurement approximately equal to the length of a forearm</note> and a width of 3 cubits, the entrance should face North. # Characteristics for Placing Rooms, Halls, Pillars, and Entrances within the House according to Length and Width

To all those who wish to measure the house site and live in joy,
Listen as I declare this science <note>Sastra</note> without any flaws;
Do not be confused in this world like a fox seeking milk in a palm tree,
May the tusked, jewel-wearing Ganesha <note>the elephant-headed deity of beginnings</note> protect us.

Seeking the help of the One who saw the Aryan Vedas <note>referring to the Creator or a divine sage</note>,
The book composed by the son of the famous Mukundan, the enemy of foes,
And the book spoken by Lord Sadashiva, who shares half his body with Goddess Uma;
<margin>( )</margin> Study these with excellence and use the measuring rod so that the house may flourish.

<margin>(128)</margin> If the measurement is seven cubits, the intended tasks will not be completed,
It will bring death to the children who come to live there;
According to the book spoken by Goddess Saraswati in clear detail,
See the Vedas to know if past karma can indeed be dissolved.

<margin>(129)</margin> A width and length of eight cubits is good for intended tasks,
No wicked person will approach; they will stay in distant lands.
There will be much prosperity, including gardens and wealth;
O one with rounded tresses, see what Lord Maheswara <note>Shiva</note> has said.

A width and length of nine cubits will cause salvation and iron <note>strength/rigidity</note> to depart,
Though one may live to age fifty, it brings death to the wife;
Lord Shambhu <note>Shiva</note> saw this and spoke; let there not be much distress,
Do not cause trouble; look instead at the book of the measurements.

<margin>131</margin> For those who affectionately set a width and length of ten cubits,
There will be a life of abundance with flocks of goats and cattle;
Great goodness will arise and agriculture will flourish;
See how the Pure One considered all these and spoke.

<margin>132</margin> If it is one more than ten <note>11 cubits</note>, listen as I speak with affection,
The mind need not be troubled; there is no evil deed or misfortune;
There will be plenty of milk, rice, and great beauty;
The Father <note>God</note> graced us with this; search and see for yourself.

<margin>123</margin>
If the measurement is ten plus two <note>original: "பத்துமேயிரண்டு" - referring to 12 'adi' or feet</note>, listen with care to what is said:
All wealth will vanish and will not remain upon this earth.
If you do not observe this closely, the square symmetry will be ruined.
Just like a divided share, great suffering and distress will follow.

<margin>124</margin>
If it is seven plus six <note>13 feet</note>, the intended works will turn out evil.
Those who lived there permanently will become enemies upon arrival.
Troubles will surround the place, and kinsmen will be lost.
There will be much downfall; behold, this is what Lord Shankara <note>The Hindu God Shiva, often cited as the primordial source of architectural knowledge</note> has declared.

<margin>125</margin>
If it is eight plus six <note>14 feet</note>, see that there shall be no pleasure.
Though there is no loss of life, our Lord Natha said it brings weakness.

Look at this law closely; no other scripture is required.

<margin>126</margin>
If the measurement arises as three times five <note>15 feet</note>, it must never be done.
It causes death through disease, and the undertaken work will be ill-fated.

<margin>127</margin>

There will be much mercy, and a life of great excellence.

<margin>128</margin>

The Lord who wears the Konrai flowers <note>Shiva</note> will grant his grace.
One will become like a king on this earth, and great luck will manifest.
Life will be led with prosperity, and wealth will be abundant.

<margin>129</margin>

Behold, Shankara said that obstacles will surely strike.

<margin>130</margin>
If it is twice eight plus one <note>17 feet - note: the text here repeats a variation or specific calculation for 17</note>, the beautiful wife or female servant will perish.
Engaging in trade on this earth will not yield profit; there is much evil.
War and the destruction of three generations of kin will lead to death.
Do not wander in confusion; behold, what Mahadeva <note>The Great God</note> has spoken.

If the measurement is seen as twenty, the elevated house will be excellent.
One will be blessed with sons and daughters, and there will be famous joys.
The luck of cattle will arrive, and heavenly blessings will be fulfilled.
The Lord with the holy matted hair <note>Shiva</note> said this will bring joy with the Goddess of Fortune.

If it is four times five plus one <note>21 feet</note>, listen as I speak of the goodness:
There will be many cows giving milk and great fortune will arise.
There is no doubt or hesitation <note>original: "சஙகர மொன்றுமில்லை"</note> regarding a prosperous life.
The Lord spoke this with joy as a means of achievement.
