# The Science of House-Building
## (3), (4), (5), (6), (7)

The sixty-four arts and sciences <note>original: "kalai-jnanam"</note>, the eighteen Puranas <note>ancient Hindu mythological texts</note>, the four Vedas, and the six Shastras <note>treatises on law and science</note> all reside here; furthermore, it is a place where cattle and various birds will gather.

***

If a flower's fragrance lingers in the direction of **Agni** <note>the South-East, governed by the Fire God</note>, the virtuous wife of the house will become barren.
If the red sky stretches long and sprouts appear,
There will be much accumulation, but also strife caused by a thief.
Behold, this is the book of victory as spoken by **Mayan** <note>the divine architect of Hindu mythology</note>.

**(The Meaning):** If a fragrant flower stands in the direction of Agni (South-East), the wife, despite her chastity, will be unable to conceive. Furthermore, there will be fear and dread caused by venomous snakes. Due to the threat of fire and the fear of thieves, wealth will not serve worthy people, and the master of the house will face ruin.

***

If the fragrance comes and stands affectionately in the corner of **Antaka** <note>Yama, the God of Death; the Southern direction</note>,
There will be much anxiety in the mind, along with arrogance and conflict.
Following this evil, there will be disease and fear.
Mayan, who composed these songs in beautiful Tamil, stated this without error.

**(The Meaning):** If the flower's scent stands in the North <note>The text mentions "Yaman's corner" but identifies it here as the North; usually, Yama governs the South. This may be a regional variation or a specific architectural orientation</note>, there will be constant worry, distress, and pressure in the mind. Conflicts with many people and wicked deeds will arise. Evils, diseases, and fears will increase.

***

If it stands in the **Kanni** corner <note>the South-West, associated with the Virgin Goddess or Nirrti</note>, there will be great possession of wealth.
Food and riches will gather, and a beautiful woman will come to stay.
Gold and grains will be plentiful, and there will be much to spend.
Behold, this is the systematic book spoken previously by Mayan.

**(The Meaning):** If the flower stands in the Kanni corner (South-West), wealth, food, and gold will abound. The radiance of Lakshmi <note>Goddess of Prosperity</note> and profit from the nine types of grain <note>navadhanya</note> will be present. Like an un-drying ocean, ancestral wealth will increase more and more every day.

***

If it stands facing the West of **Varuna** <note>the God of the Oceans and the West</note>,
Great hidden treasures and wealth will remain, and past sins will vanish.
They will live receiving the fruits of the works they intended to do.
Mayan, who possesses the skill of storytelling, said this will bring prosperity.

**(The Meaning):** If the beautiful and fragrant flower stands in the corner of Varuna (West), not only will immense wealth remain constant, but the intended tasks and the businesses started will result in victory.

***

If it comes to stand in the **Vayu** corner <note>the North-West, governed by the God of Wind</note>,
One will die from bodily disease, and wealth will be lost even in dreams.
Ghosts will take possession, and the place will become a cremation ground.
Even the children will perish; so said the successful Mayan.

# <term>Manaiyadi Sastram</term> <note>The Science of House-Ground; a traditional Tamil manual on Vastu Shastra/architecture.</note>
->Page 12<-

## Characteristics of Anthills
<note>In Tamil tradition, the presence of an anthill on a plot is a significant omen depending on its compass direction.</note>

> Wealth in the East, the house crumbles in the South-East;
> Sorrow in the South, and goodness in the South-West;
> Children are gained in the West, and great misery in the North-West;
> Prosperity in the North, disease in the North-East;
> And death if the anthill rises in the center.

(Meaning): If an anthill appears in the direction of **Indra** (East), wealth will increase. In the direction of **Agni** (South-East), the house will crumble and fall into ruin. In the direction of **Yama** (South), sorrow will occur. In the direction of **Nirriti** (South-West), goodness will result. In the direction of **Isanya** (North-East), disease will occur. In the direction of **Varuna** (West), the blessing of children will be obtained. In the direction of **Vayu** (North-West), there will be great grief. In the direction of **Kubera** (North), there will be great benefit. If it occurs in the **Brahma-sthana** (the center of the plot), it signifies death. <meta>catchword: (12)</meta>

---

## Characteristics of Wall Construction

For a wall with a width of one <term>muzham</term> <note>a cubit; roughly 18 inches</note>, the height should be two cubits. A wall height of [9] <term>saan</term> <note>a span; the distance from thumb to pinky</note> is for Kings and Brahmins. A wall height of [8] spans is for Vaishyas and Shudras. A wall height of [7] spans is for those of mixed castes. The height should not be less than these measurements. This must be understood before building. If a wall falls while being built, there is a fear of theft. If the wall falls outward, there will be conflict. If it falls inward, it is inauspicious for the owner of the house.

---

## Installation of the Foundation

The foundation pillar should not be installed on the days of <term>Nandai, Irundhai, or Pathiri</term> <note>specific lunar phases/tithis</note>, nor on the New Moon or Full Moon. It may be done on other days.

---

## Results of the Days of the Week

**Sunday:** Danger from fire.
**Monday:** Conflict.
**Tuesday:** Fear of death.
**Wednesday, Thursday, and Friday:** If done on these three days, auspiciousness will result.
**Saturday:** Loss of grain/food.

---

## Results of the Nakshatras <note>Lunar Mansions/Star Signs</note>

**Hastam, Revati, Ashwini, Satayam, Avittam, Pushyam, Thiruvonam, Punarpoosam, Rohini, Chithirai, Anusham, Uthiram, Uthiradam, and Uthirattadhi:** If the installation is performed under these stars, wealth and the joy of children will be attained.

---

## Determination of Auspicious Planets and Zodiac Signs

If auspicious planets are in the 4th, 10th, 7th, 1st, or 9th houses, or if one observes the strength of auspicious planets, or if an auspicious planet is in its exalted state, or if a malefic planet is in the 6th, 3rd, or 11th houses—performing the installation at such a time will bring auspicious results.

<header>The Science of Architecture</header>

# Characteristics of the Time-Serpent’s Movement in the Months

<note>The "Time-Serpent" (Kalasarpa) is a symbolic energy grid that rotates through the cardinal directions based on the sun's position in the zodiac. Architects must track its head, body, and tail to avoid "piercing" the wrong part of the serpent when planting the main pillar.</note>

**Movement of the Time-Serpent:** In the three <term>Sankrantis</term> <note>The transition of the sun from one zodiac sign to another</note> of **Taurus, Gemini, and Cancer**, the head of the Time-Serpent stays in the **Northeast** <note>Ishanya</note>, the waist in the **Northwest** <note>Vayu</note>, and the tail in the **Southwest** <note>Niruti</note>. Following this, in the three transitions of **Leo, Virgo, and Libra**, the head moves to the **Northwest**, the waist to the **Southwest**, and the tail to the **Southeast** <note>Agni</note>. Following this, in the three transitions of **Scorpio, Sagittarius, and Capricorn**, the head is in the **Southwest**, the waist in the **Southeast**, and the tail in the **Northeast**. Following this, in the three transitions of **Aquarius, Pisces, and Aries**, the head is in the **Southeast**, the waist in the **Northeast**, and the tail in the **Northwest**.

If one performs the <term>Stambha Pratishta</term> <note>The ritual installation of the main structural pillar</note> in this manner, it brings prosperity. If done on the **head**, it causes death; if done on the **back**, it brings progress; if done on the **tail**, it causes instability. Installing the pillar according to these positions will bring great wealth.

## Determination of the Sun’s Transition

When the Sun is in the transitions of **Taurus, Cancer, or Gemini**, and even if it is aligned with **Virgo, Leo, or Libra**, if the Moon is in the <term>Nakshatras</term> <note>Lunar mansions/stars</note> of **Uttara Phalguni, Hasta, or Chitra**, the pillar installation can be performed in the **Southeast**. When the Sun is in the transitions of **Aquarius, Pisces, or Aries**, and even if it is aligned with **Taurus, Gemini, or Cancer**, if the Moon is in the stars of **Rohini, Mrigashira, or Punarvasu**, the pillar installation should be performed in the **Southwest**. 

Performing the installation according to these rules brings auspiciousness. If done in other months, it will bring ruin. If the pillar is installed when the <term>Lagna</term> <note>Ascendant/Zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon</note> is strong, or when Mercury or Venus is in the rising sign, that house will have no fear of fire, thieves, or the government. If the house is built on a Friday when the Moon is in a stable sign or in the 5th or 6th house, wealth will flourish.

## Now, the Royal Yoga (Auspicious Alignments)

On a Thursday, if Jupiter or Venus is in the rising signs of **Cancer, Pisces, or Sagittarius**, building a house at that time will allow the inhabitants to live happily like kings, blessed with children and prosperity. If a benefic planet is in the 7th house or aspecting <note>looking at</note> the rising sign, installing the pillar at such a time is highly auspicious. If Venus is in Pisces, or if Jupiter is in the 4th house, or if Saturn is in its position of exaltation in the 10th or 7th house, installing the pillar in such a rising sign will bring all-around goodness.

<summary>This page details the ritual movement of the "Time-Serpent" across the seasons and provides specific astrological configurations for the auspicious installation of a building's foundation pillar.</summary>

# The Position of the Sun Month by Month

### Venpa Verse <note>A classical form of Tamil poetry</note>

> Success and fulfillment shall flourish in the mind,
> Like the sacred waters of the Ganges flowing through the heart—
> If one follows the path of the months without error,
> The celestial light shall crown the completed dwelling.

---(*)---

## Determining the Status of the Birth Star

Count from your birth star <term>Janma Nakshatra</term> to the current star, multiply by four, and add the lunar day <term>Tithi</term> and the day of the week <term>Vara</term>. The positions of the nine planets <term>Navagrahas</term> are as follows: 110—The Sun. The Moon brings gain and prosperity. Mars, Mercury, and Jupiter also bring profit. Venus brings comfort, while Saturn, Rahu, and Ketu have their specific influences on the lunar day's results.

If Jupiter or Venus is positioned in the first house <term>Lagnam</term> at the time of building, the inhabitants will live in happiness for five hundred years. If Mercury is in the first house, they will thrive for fifty years. If Mercury, Venus, and Jupiter are all in the first house together, it ensures fifty years of prosperity. If Jupiter is in the first house, and the Moon, Mars, and Saturn are in the fourth house, the home will be auspicious for many years. If Venus alone is in the first house, it grants one hundred years of stability.

When the Sun is in the zodiac signs of Leo, Virgo, or Libra, the foundational pillar <term>Stambha Pratishta</term> must be installed in the Northeast <term>Ishanya</term> corner. <note>Failure to do so results in "Kumbha Dosha" or a spiritual flaw in the vessel of the home.</note> When the Sun is in these two signs <note>referring to specific solar transitions</note>, the pillar must be consecrated according to the sacred rites. When the Sun is in Taurus, Gemini, or Cancer, the pillar must be installed in the Southeast <term>Agni</term> corner. These rules must be followed for weddings and other foundational ceremonies. If one builds without observing these measurements and timings, poverty and sorrow will follow, and the lifespan of the master of the house will be diminished.

## Characteristics of Placing the Doorways

If a house has doorways placed on all four sides, it is called "The Face of the Universe" <term>Visvathomukham</term>. <note>This is considered a highly spiritual but complex layout.</note> If there are no doorways on one side but they exist on the other three, it is called "The Tiger’s Foot" <term>Vyaghrapadam</term>; the result of this is the fear of loss regarding cattle, children, and threats from thieves.

If there is no doorway facing South, but doors exist on the other three sides, it is called "The Giver of Fortune" <term>Bhagyapradam</term>. If there is no doorway facing West, but doors exist on the other three sides, it is called "The Abode of Prosperity" <term>Sri Nilayam</term>; the result is that the inhabitants will live very well. If there is no doorway facing North, but doors exist on the other three sides, it is called "The Abode of the Sun" <term>Suryanilayam</term>; the result of this is "fear of the King" <note>likely meaning legal troubles or issues with authorities</note> and various difficulties.

<summary>This page outlines the astrological requirements for house construction, focusing on the positioning of the Sun and planets to ensure longevity, and the spiritual consequences of doorway placement.</summary>
<keywords>Vastu Shastra, architecture, astrology, Navagrahas, birth stars, foundational pillar, doorway placement, Sri Nilayam, prosperity</keywords>

<page-num>81</page-num>
<header>The Science of Architecture,</header>

If there are no walls on the shark-faced <note>likely referring to a specific decorative or structural projection</note> side, but there is a wall in the center of the conch-shell corner, it is called *Pundarika* <note>Lotus-type</note>. Its result is mediocre.

### Characteristics of Spacing

A house built with walls on the South and West is called *Kamalakaram* <note>Lotus-source</note> in terms of its spacing. Its result is the primary blessing of Lakshmi <note>Goddess of wealth</note>. A house built with the West face closed and the <unclear>dark</unclear> face built is called *Swarnapalan* <note>Gold-result</note> in its spacing; its result will be fear of theft and suffering. A house built on the North and East sides is called *Pushkatamushtikam* in its spacing; its result is fear of theft and fear of poison. For a house built on the South, West, and North, if the doorway is placed on the East, it is called *Putravirutti* <note>Increase of progeny</note>; its result is fearlessness. For a house built on the North, East, and South, if the doorway is placed on the West, it is called *Atishayam* <note>The Marvelous</note>; its result is loss. For a house built on the East, West, and North, if the doorway is placed on the North, there will be good health. If there are doorways on three sides—West, East, and North—it is called *Kalyanapadam* <note>The State of Marriage/Auspiciousness</note>; wealth will be attained in this. If houses are built on all four sides—East, South, West, and North—it is called *Chatursala* <note>Four-hall/Courtyard house</note>; its result is excellent.

### Results of the Nakshatras <note>Lunar Mansions/Stars</note>

Ashwini, Mrigashirsha, Rohini, Pushya, Hasta, Chitra, Anuradha, Uttarashada, Shravana, Dhanishta, Shatabhisha—these 11 stars are **Excellent**.

Ardra, Punarvasu, Uttara Phalguni, Jyeshtha, Uttara Bhadrapada, Revati—these 6 stars are **Mediocre**.

Bharani, Krittika, Ashlesha, Magha, Purva Phalguni, Purva Bhadrapada, Swati, Purvashada, Vishakha, Mula—these 10 stars are **Inferior**.

## Results of the Tara <note>Strength of the Star</note> for Stars and Yogas

For Ashwini, it is Sheshi-tara. For Purvabhadra, it is Peshi-tara.
For Rohini, it is Sari-tara. Since Ashwini is also Peshi-tara—
For Punarvasu, it is Peshi-tara. In that star <unclear>illegible</unclear>
For Magha, it is Sari-tara. Star—20. If added,
For Hasta, it is Peshi-tara—2-8—interior—14th.
For Vishakha, it is Sari-tara. Therefore, starting from Vishakambam,
the 14 Yogas will come to Ashwini.
For Shravana, it is Sari-tara.
Since Rohini is the 4th—Sari-tara—the meaning is the second Yoga
will come. In this manner, check the calculations and understand the relationship
between the Star and the Yoga.
Similarly, counting from Bharani for the next 9 stars as mentioned,
one should know the Yoga.

# The Science of Sculpture

The <term>Chatushpadam</term> <note>A four-part division or square base in architectural planning</note> and the <term>Kimastughnam</term> <note>One of the eleven Karana or astrological time-segments</note>—these five are considered lost or unfavorable. Among the <term>Amshas</term> <note>Astrological portions or degrees</note>, the Middle Amsha and the Karuva Amsha are superior; the Gosara Amsha is known as the "Son Amsha." In this manner, there is no increase or decrease beyond one <term>Angulam</term> <note>A traditional unit of measurement, roughly the width of a thumb</note> or one Amsha. 

The number 39 is auspicious, while 9 is not. If the Amshas do not align, it is unfavorable. If the patron (owner) is over 60 years of age, it brings fortune; if under 60, it brings loss. If built under the influence of the Lord Indra, it is auspicious; if under the Lord of Fire (Agni), it will cause the structure to be built upside down or in reverse order.

### Rules for Cutting Trees

One must understand the <term>Varam</term> (day), <term>Yogam</term>, and <term>Karanam</term> <note>Components of the Hindu calendar/Panchangam</note>. After checking the strength of the Moon's position for the patron, one must give the carpenter sandalwood paste, betel leaves, flowers, and a ritual fee (<term>Dakshina</term>). Only then should they be sent to the forest. Cutting trees in this manner brings prosperity.

### Determining the Lunar Day for Cutting Trees

If cut during the five days starting from <term>Panchami</term> <note>The fifth lunar day</note>, the wood will not be infested by insects. If the <term>Rohini</term> star falls within these five days, it is most excellent; even the Acacia tree (<term>Vela</term>), if cut then, will remain pest-free. 

**Days to avoid:** Trees should not be cut when the Sun is in the <term>Krittika</term> star, nor when Venus is in the third or fourth quarters of <term>Shravana</term> and <term>Dhanishta</term>, or the third quarter of <term>Shatabhisha</term> and <term>Purva Bhadrapada</term>.

Even if the trees were cut previously, they must not be brought into the house when these stars are presiding. Furthermore, trees should not be cut—nor brought into the house—when the Moon is in these eleven stars: <term>Shravana, Dhanishta, Shatabhisha, Purva Bhadrapada, Uttara Bhadrapada, Magha, Purva Phalguni, Uttara Phalguni, Hasta, Chitra,</term> and <term>Swati</term>.

### Verse on Determining the Womb/Foundation Depth

> When the Sun and Saturn are in Sagittarius, at thirteen and three-quarters...
> The womb of the house is formed at six and a quarter—O beautiful one, listen!
> From Cancer to Scorpio, the leg is a quarter-sixteenth,
> In the sign of Sagittarius, it is said to be one part.

### Characteristics of the <term>Sankusthapana</term>

<term>Sankusthapana</term> <note>The ritual installation of the "Sanku" or gnomon/foundation pillar</note>: If this has been performed previously at a site, one may build the house at their convenience; there is no defect in this. The ritual should be performed during the waxing moon (<term>Shukla Paksha</term>) for auspicious results. It should not be performed on <term>Prathama</term> (1st), <term>Shashti</term> (6th), or <term>Ekadashi</term> (11th) lunar days, nor on the New Moon. 

Performing this on Wednesday, Thursday, or Friday brings auspicious results. It should not be done on Saturday, Sunday, or Tuesday.

### Stars for Determining the Foundation Pillar Ritual

Shatabhisha, Shravana, Uttara Phalguni, Hasta, Revati, Swati, Rohini, Mrigashira, Chitra, Pushya, Uttara Bhadrapada, Punarvasu, and Anuradha.

# The Science of House Completion

<note>original: "Manai-mudi-shastram"</note>

Regarding the stars Avittam and [unclear]—performing the *Sanku-sthapana* <note>The ritual installation of the 'Sanku' or ceremonial foundation pillar/peg</note> during these two constellations is beneficial, provided these stars are free from any astrological defects.

One must understand the various 'bases' or foundations: the base of fire, the base of auspiciousness, the base of purity, the base of disease, the base of true life, and the base of Shiva. The *Sanku-sthapana* must be performed specifically in the base of Shiva. If performed in the base of fire, the deity of fire will prevail; in the base of auspiciousness, there will be conflict; in the base of purity, poverty; and in the base of true life, destruction will occur.

### The Virgin Time-Wheel Chart

<note>original: "Kannika-kala-chakram"</note>

The base of Shiva brings the presence of Lakshmi <note>Goddess of wealth and prosperity</note>. For this house, one must draw the Virgin Time-Wheel Chart. Draw three vertical lines <note>representing the Lingam or divine pillar</note> and cross them with horizontal lines. From the base to the top, write the 27 stars starting from Ashwini across the three worlds.

->The Virgin Wheel<-

| | | |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| [Diagrammatic text] | Shiva, A | |
| 2nd Quarter | Ro, ✱ | Sun, Moon, Mars |
| 3rd Quarter | Time, Mercury | 2 Times, Art |
| 4th Quarter | Pa, Pu, Pu | A, Pu |
| A, Aa, Ma | Ke, Re | |

Count and observe this wheel. In whichever cross-line the Moon resides, if the Sun is also on that line, there is a fear of fire. If Mars is there, there is a fear of death. If Mercury is there, fear of disease. If Jupiter is there, loss of grain. If Venus is there, quarrels. If Saturn is there, fear of theft. If Rahu is there, bodily suffering. If Ketu is there, a corpse <note>symbolizing extreme misfortune or death in the house</note>. 

When calculating this Virgin Wheel, if the star is "facing upwards" <note>Urdhva-mukha</note> at the time of the pillar installation, it is most excellent. One must not install the pillar when the star is "facing downwards" <note>Adho-mukha</note>. The stars Rohini, Pushya, Arudra, Shravana, Dhanishta, Shatabhisha, Uttara Phalguni, Uttarashada, and Uttarabhadrapada are the great "upward-facing" stars. Therefore, the pillar installation can be done during these times. Furthermore, during these times, one may build balconies, upper floors, cowsheds, and temple towers.

->One may light the ceremonial fire during "three-faced" stars<-

->One may purchase cows, goats, cattle, and horses<-

The stars are Revati, Hasta, Punarvasu, Chitra, Swati, Ashwini, and Mrigashira...

Anusham and Kettai <note>Anusham (Anuradha) and Kettai (Jyeshtha) are specific lunar mansions or stars in Indian astrology.</note>—these stars are excellent for erecting walls and for purchasing elephants, horses, goats, and cattle.

Under the "Downward-Facing Stars" <note>original: "Adhomukha Nakshatra"</note>, one may dig wells, ponds, and lakes. Vishakam, Mulam, Ayilyam, Karthigai, Puram, Puradam, Purattathi, Magam, and Bharani—these nine stars are downward-facing. # Science of House Building
<note>original: "Manaiyadi Sastram" — a traditional manual on Vastu Shastra, the ancient Indian system of architecture.</note>

## Viruttam
<note>A type of poetic meter in Tamil literature used here to convey technical rules.</note>

For a house of world-renowned fame, divide the plot into eight-by-eight squares <note>totaling 64 squares</note>. In the center, take four squares for the Creator <note>Brahma</note>; four squares for the corners are assigned to the spirits; the four directions and the remaining eight squares are for the divine lords. O lady as slender as a flowering branch, accept this as the virtuous way. (14)

->The characteristics for building a house<-
->within the sixty-four divisions of a plot<-

Examine the four boundaries of the interior space,
Divide it clearly into sixty-four parts,
It is said the four central parts are the Station of Brahma,
The four directions are the auspicious parts of the beginning.

(Meaning) Measure the house site into four main sections and subdivide those into sixty-four equal shares. The four shares in the very center are the **Station of Brahma** <term>Brahma-sthanam: the central, open core of a traditional house believed to be the seat of the creator</term>. The four cardinal directions are the primary divisions. (14)

Listen as I describe the nature of those four directions,
They are known as the **Portion of Vishnu** <note>The Preserver</note>;
Know that all the remaining areas are the **Portion of the Devas** <note>Celestial beings</note>;
This is the way the joyful Mayans <note>Mayan: the divine architect of Hindu mythology</note> spoke in pure Tamil.

(Meaning) The four cardinal directions are the Portion of Vishnu, and all other remaining sections are the Portion of the Devas. This is the statement clearly spoken by the sage Mayan for the benefit of all. (15)

The physical form <note>Pindam</note> is granted in accordance with the movement of the universe <note>Andam</note>;
The house is constructed in accordance with that physical form;
With eyes, nose, and ears properly aligned,
The house becomes a living being with a soul.

(Meaning) Just as the human body <note>the microcosm</note> corresponds to the universe <note>the macrocosm</note>, the house must correspond to the human body. A house built with proper proportions of limbs and features is a living home. (16)

If built in this excellent way, the foundation will be firm;
The Goddess of Wealth <note>Lakshmi</note> will reside there in her radiant form;
The lineage will flourish, cattle will multiply, and gold will accumulate;
Mayan, having examined the truths, said a beautiful life is certain.

(Meaning) If a house is built according to these proportions, its foundation will be strong. It will prosper daily with wealth and livestock. In such a house, the Goddess Lakshmi will dwell and perform her dance of joy. (17)

## Venba
<note>A classical Tamil poetic form known for its strict rules and brevity.</note>

Establish the four central pillars of wisdom, place the weight toward the direction of Vishnu, and ensure the light of the rising sun reaches the silver-white spaces...

<summary>This page details the "Padavinyasa" or the grid-based layout of a house, dividing a plot into 64 squares and assigning them to deities like Brahma and Vishnu. It emphasizes that a house is a living organism (Pindam) that must reflect the harmony of the universe (Andam) to ensure the prosperity of its inhabitants.</summary>

### If the Minister is born, the house on this earth
### Will flourish with honors, like the King of Heaven,

(Meaning) If the pillars and foundations are set when the Moon is rising, the expanse of the roof joined when Mercury is rising, the wealth or harmony established when Venus is rising, and the ceremonial entry <note>The 'Grihapravesha' or house-warming ceremony</note> performed when Jupiter is rising, the inhabitants will live with divine prosperity. (18)

->Days on which auspicious works should not be done<-

Three days every month, fifteen days at the end of every year, seven days before and after the occurrence of an eclipse, and the days of the "Eye-Opening" <note>Specific astrological junctions considered volatile</note>—the ancient sages of the Vedas have declared these times to be evil and to be avoided.

->Knowing the "Eye-Sight" of the house based on the day of the week<-

# Venpa Verse

Starting from the Sun, calculate with care,
Count from the Horse <note>The star Ashwini</note> as you go—
One for a single eye, two for double sight,
The rest are blind, as the sages know.

(Meaning) Count from the day of the week the house construction begins back to Sunday. Multiply that sum by 3 and 7. With that total, count through the stars starting from Ashwini. If it falls within the first 6 days, it is "Single-Eyed"; for the next 12 days, it is "Two-Eyed"; for the 6 days after that, it is "Blind." One must ensure the house is built during a "sighted" star alignment. (19)

->Knowing the stages of the house: Infancy, Youth, Old Age, and Death<-

Multiply the calculated "Anguish" <term>Amsha: a mathematical unit in Vastu astrology</term> total by 7 and divide by 5. The remainder indicates the stage: 1 is Infancy (Balas), 2 is Youth (Yauvana), 3 is Maturity (Kaumara), 4 is Old Age (Muppu), and 5 is Death (Marana).

### The Meaning of the Kalamrita Verses

The position of the stars when deciding to build a house:
When establishing the foundation and fixing the pillars, if the Sun is in Krittika, Bharani (4th quarter), or Rohini (1st quarter); or if the Moon is in Krittika; or if Mars is in any of these seven stars: Krittika, Magha, Revati, Uttara Ashadha, Hasta, Mula, or Pushya—it will bring extreme sorrow to the master of the house.

That house will be reduced to ashes by fire. Furthermore, if Saturn is in any of these 6 stars: Anuradha, Jyeshtha, Swati, Purva Bhadrapada, Uttara Bhadrapada, or Bharani—whatever house is built will quickly become a dwelling place for demons and restless spirits <note>Brahmarakshasa: a powerful, scholarly ghost</note>. On the other hand, if auspicious planets...

Whether it brings fortune or misfortune, if a planet is in an enemy's house <note>astrological sign</note> and occupies the 10th or 7th house from the Ascendant <note>Lagnam</note>, that house will fall into the hands of others. If Mercury is positioned in any of these six stars—Chitra, Ashwini, Uttiram, Hastam, Mrigashira, or Rohini—the master of the house will obtain great wealth <term>Aishwarya: prosperity and material wealth</term>.

If Jupiter is in any of these nine stars—Rohini, Pushya, Mrigashira, Ayilyam, Shravana, Uttiram, Uttiradam, or Puradam—and resides in the 10th house, there will be the blessing of children. If Venus is in any of these six—Arudra, Punarvasu, Dhanishta, Vishakha, Shatabhisha, or Chitra—there will be an abundance of grain. If Venus is in the 7th house, the growth of grain stores is assured.

# The Characteristics of Income, Expenditure, and Age for a House
### Definition

To build a house where Lakshmi <note>Goddess of wealth</note> resides, listen to the traditional rules of the land. Being a man of virtue, take a measuring rod of four hand-spans <note>one 'saan' is the span of an outstretched hand</note> and measure the four sides of the chosen plot. Multiply the length and width to find the area in 'kuzhi' <note>a traditional unit of area</note>, and then divide that area by eight to determine the 'Ayam' <term>Ayam: a mathematical formula used to determine the spiritual 'income' or vibration of a building</term>.

### Kali Viruttam <note>A specific meter of Tamil poetry</note>

> With a rod measuring four hand-spans,
> Seek the boundaries for the fragrant house;
> When the eastern door is set in flourishing beauty,
> Calculate the Ayam according to the proper grace.

(Meaning) Using a rod that measures four hand-spans of the master's hand, measure the East, West, North, and South. Multiply the dimensions to find the area (kuzhi), and use this for the Ayam calculations to ensure the house matches the cardinal directions.

### Venpa <note>A classical form of Tamil stanza</note>

> Take the four-span rod and without error,
> Measure the four boundaries without slanting;
> Multiply the area, O Lady of this Earth, and if you divide by eight,
> The remainder will reveal the 'Womb' <note>Garbha: the vital center or dimensions</note> of the house.

<summary>This page details the astrological influences of Mercury, Jupiter, and Venus on a household and introduces the "Sanat Kumara" system for measuring a building site using a four-span rod to ensure prosperity and safety from fire.</summary>
<keywords>Vastu Shastra, Tamil architecture, astrology, planetary positions, Ayam calculation, Sanat Kumara, measurement units, house building rituals</keywords>

<header>The Science of Sculpture (Shilpa Shastra)</header>

### Verse

->Multiply the area by twelve and divide by eight for the remainder<-
->Multiply by nine and divide by ten for the outcome<-
->Multiply by three and divide by eight for the Yoni<-
->Multiply by eight and divide by twenty-seven for the Star<-
->Multiply by four and divide by thirty for the Lunar Day<-
->Multiply by eight and divide by seven for the Weekday<-
->Multiply by seven and divide by twelve for the Zodiac Sign<-
->Multiply by nine and divide by four for the Caste<-
->Multiply by eight and divide by nine for the Part<-
->Multiply by twenty-seven and divide by one hundred for the Age<-

In the texts of *Mayan*, *Sanatkumara Vastu*, *Kadaka Vastu*, *Vimeshura Maalai*, *Ullamudaiyan*, and *Kaalamirtham* <note>These are classical Sanskrit and Tamil treatises on architecture and astrology</note>, various measurements for a "Divine Foot" are mentioned, such as 34, 22, or 25 inches <term>Angulam (inch)</term>. It is also suggested that when a homeowner builds a house, they should use the span of their own hand or forearm <term>Muzham (cubit)</term>. However, upon consideration, these varied measurements often lead to inconsistencies. Therefore, one should primarily use the "Standard Foot" <term>Jaathi Adi</term> currently mandated by the government and prevalent across all regions for measuring land and plots. 

First, measure the total area (length multiplied by width) of the proposed building within the plot—oriented East-West and South-North. Ensure the measurements are accurate and consistent. This total area, known as the *Dhruva* (constant), is then used to calculate the *Garbha Aya* (the womb/essence of the house) through the following eight calculations:

->**First**<-
Take the total area (square footage) as the constant, divide it by 8; the remainder is the **Garbha Aya** <note>The "income" or foundational energy of the space</note>.

->**Second**<-
Multiply the area by 8 and divide by 12; the remainder is **Adayam** (Profit/Gain).

->**Third**<-
Multiply the area by 9 and divide by 10; the remainder is **Virayam** (Expenditure/Loss).

->**Fourth**<-
Multiply the area by 3 and divide by 8; the remainder is **Yoni** <note>Directional energy or "birth-place" of the structure</note>.

->**Fifth**<-
Multiply the area by 8 and divide by 27; the remainder is **Nakshatra** (Birth Star).

->**Sixth**<-
Multiply the area by 4 and divide by 30; the remainder is **Tithi** (Lunar Day).

->**Seventh**<-
Multiply the area by 8 and divide by 7; the remainder is **Vaaram** (Day of the Week).

->**Eighth**<-
Multiply the area by 7 and divide by 12; the remainder is **Rasi** (Zodiac Sign).

<summary>This page outlines the mathematical formulas (Ayaadi Shadvarga) used in traditional Vastu Shastra to ensure a building's dimensions harmonize with cosmic energies. It advocates for using a standardized "Government Foot" over variable historical measurements to ensure accuracy.</summary>

<keywords>Vastu Shastra, Ayaadi calculations, architecture, Mayan, Garbha Aya, traditional measurement, Tamil construction, astrology</keywords>

# The Science of House Building
<note>original: "Manaiyadi Shastram" — a traditional Tamil manual for Vastu Shastra, focusing on the measurements of a house to ensure the well-being of its inhabitants.</note>

## Ninth
Multiply by 9 and divide by 4; the remaining balance is the **Lineage**.
<note>original: "Vamsham" — refers to the social or spiritual lineage associated with the measurement.</note>

## Tenth
Multiply by 6 and divide by 6; the remaining balance is the **Quality**.
<note>original: "Amsham" — a structural or astrological division.</note>

## Eleventh
Multiply by 27 and divide by 7; the remaining balance is the **Age**.
<note>original: "Vayathu" — refers to the predicted lifespan or duration of the building's prosperity.</note>

### Regarding the 11 Suitabilities:

If the **Income** <note>Ayakam</note> has no remainder, take it as 8.
If the **Profit** <note>Adayam</note> has no remainder, take it as 12.
If the **Expenditure** <note>Vinayam</note> has no remainder, take it as 10.
If the **Yoni** <note>Directional Source</note> has no remainder, take it as 8.
If the **Star** <note>Nalam/Nakshatra</note> has no remainder, take it as 27.
If the **Lunar Day** <note>Tithi</note> has no remainder, take it as 30.
If the **Day of the Week** <note>Varam</note> has no remainder, take it as 7.
If the **Zodiac Sign** <note>Rasi</note> has no remainder, take it as 12.
If the **Lineage** <note>Vamsham</note> has no remainder, take it as 4.
If the **Quality** <note>Amsham</note> has no remainder, take it as 6.
If the **Age** <note>Vayathu</note> has no remainder, take it as 7.

If the **Profit** is high and the **Expenditure** is low, the owner of the house will become wealthy.

If the **Profit** is low and the **Expenditure** is high, he will become impoverished.

If the **Profit** and **Expenditure** are equal, his status will remain average.

Divide the first step called the **Fixed Base** <term>Dhruvasayam: a fundamental calculation constant</term> by 8; taking the remaining total, the results are as follows:

| Number | Result | Deity |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| If 1 comes | Flag Result | Indra |
| If 2 comes | Smoke Result | Agni <note>The Fire God</note> |
| If 3 comes | Lion Result | Maya <note>The Divine Architect</note> |
| If 4 comes | Dog Result | Nirriti <note>Deity of Sorrow/Southwest</note> |
| If 5 comes | Bull Result | Varuna <note>God of Water/West</note> |
| If 6 comes | Crow Result | Vayu <note>God of Wind/Northwest</note> |
| If 7 comes | Elephant Result | Kubera <note>God of Wealth/North</note> |
| If 8 comes | Crow/Raven Result | Ishanya <note>Deity of the Northeast</note> |

The **Flag Result** <note>Dhuvajayam</note> can be built facing North.
The **Lion Result** <note>Simmayam</note> can be built facing South or West.
The **Bull Result** <note>Rishabayam</note> [facing] West; however, if it results in [certain unfavorable measures], it should not be built in that spot.
The **Flag Result** should not be <unclear>built</unclear> in the Southeast corner <note>Agni-moolai</note>.
[The building] should not be constructed on any other lunar days.
In whichever other direction it has been [properly] started, it may be completed accordingly; it should not be built in any other places.

It emphasizes that profit must exceed expenditure for a homeowner's prosperity and gives specific directional restrictions for construction.</summary>

<page-num>108</page-num>
<header>Science of Sculpture and Architecture</header>

The *Vrushabha Aya* <note>The 'Bull' dimension, associated with stability</note> and *Simha Aya* <note>The 'Lion' dimension, associated with power</note> are suitable for Brahmins and Kshatriyas <note>The priestly and warrior/governing classes</note>.
The *Gaja Aya* <note>The 'Elephant' dimension, associated with wealth</note> and *Vrushabha Aya* are suitable for Vaishyas and Shudras <note>The merchant and laboring classes</note>.
The *Dhvaja Aya* <note>The 'Flag' dimension</note> brings great merit and virtue.
The *Dhuma Aya* <note>The 'Smoke' dimension</note> brings death.
The *Simha Aya* brings poison <note>Likely metaphorical for toxicity or danger</note>.
The *Shvana Aya* <note>The 'Dog' dimension</note> brings disease.
The *Vrushabha Aya* brings demonic influences <note>original: 'Rakshasa-pratham'</note>.
The *Gaja Aya* brings happiness.
The *Kaka Aya* <note>The 'Crow' dimension</note> brings the strength of the bull.
To understand the qualities of these *Ayas* when determining measurements: if there is an excess of one inch, know that the full benefit will be attained. If there is a remainder, the results will be balanced. If the inches are deficient, know that the qualities will be unfavorable. Here are the details to understand the qualities of these *Ayas*:

### Strength of the Lunar Days (Tithi)

For *Dhuma Aya*, the quality is 4; for *Simha Aya*, the quality is 1; for *Shvana Aya*, the quality is 4; for *Vrushabha Aya*, the quality is 7; for *Gaja Aya*, the quality is 5; for *Kaka Aya*, the quality is 8.

In these *Ayas*, there should be no deficiency in qualities.
The New Moon <note>Amavasya</note> and the following first lunar day <note>Prathama</note> bring suffering. The fourth, ninth, and fourteenth lunar days—these three will cause the house to suffer. On the New Moon, there is a fear of death for the inhabitants.

### The First Chapter on Aya: A Venpa Verse

*In five, they shall rule as kings; in six, great sorrow;*
*In three, the lion's power; in one, the womb's beginning;*
*Four, eight, and two bring destruction to the house;*
*To say seven and eight is the ordained rule.*

(Meaning): To know the *Garbha* <note>The 'womb' or internal spatial capacity of a building</note> status: measure the East, West, South, and North using a four-span rod based on the master's hand-span. Multiply the resulting area and divide by eight. 
If the remainder is **five**, it is *Subha Garbha* (Auspicious Womb); auspicious deeds will flourish there. 
If **six** remains, it is *Kaka Garbha* (Crow Womb); it is mediocre and suitable only for the lowly. 
If **one** remains, it is *Garuda Garbha* (Eagle Womb); it is suitable for all four classes. 
If **three** remains, it is *Simha Garbha* (Lion Womb); it is excellent. 
If **seven** remains, it is *Gaja Garbha* (Elephant Womb); it is auspicious. 
If **two** remains, it is *Puraka Garbha* (Pigeon Womb); it is mediocre and suitable for hunters. 
If **four** remains, it is *Shvana Garbha* (Dog Womb); it is mediocre and suitable for nomads and singers. 
If **eight** remains, it is *Kadhidha Garbha* (Donkey Womb); it is mediocre; the house will not be completed, or if completed, it will fall into ruin. It is suitable for those living in huts, or for stables for peacocks and deer.

### 2. Income (Adayam)

Multiply the established base measurement <note>original: "Aayapada"; the calculated perimeter or area unit used in Vastu</note> by eight, and divide the resulting total by twelve. The remainder that stands is declared by the sages to be the "Income."

<margin>(22)</margin>
(Meaning) Take the previously mentioned base measurement, multiply it by eight, and divide the sum by twelve. The remainder is the Income.

If the remainder is one or two, great enjoyment and fortune will occur.
If it is three or four, fame and valor will be attained.
If it is five or six, wealth and grain <note>signifying agricultural prosperity</note> will abound.
If it is seven or eight, auspiciousness and true wisdom will be gained.

(Meaning) If the Income calculation results in one or two, there will be singular enjoyment of pleasures. If three or four, fame and bravery will manifest. If five or six, wealth and food supplies will increase. If seven or eight, auspiciousness and spiritual realization will be attained.

If nine, there will be natural enjoyment.
If ten, there will be influence over others.
If eleven, there will be righteous charity.
If twelve, there will be total goodness in all things.

<margin>(24)</margin>
(Meaning) If the result is nine, there will be great enjoyment and fortune. If ten, all matters will become favorable and under one's influence. If eleven, a charitable disposition will arise. If twelve, everything will result in the highest possible benefit.

### 3. Expenditure (Virayam)

To find the expenditure, multiply the base measurement by nine.
Divide that by ten; the remainder is the loss.
If the remainder is one, wealth will diminish.
If it is two, the house will be consumed by fire.

<margin>(25)</margin>
(Meaning) Multiply the base measurement by nine and divide by ten. If the remainder is one, wealth will decrease. If the remainder is two, the house is at risk of fire.

If the remainder is three, there will be good happiness.
If four, there will be an increase in children.
If five, there will be worldly success <note>The verse suggests success, while the commentary suggests waste; usually, five in this system implies "Viyaya" or loss</note>.
If six, there will be health.
If seven, there will be poverty.

(Meaning) If three remains, there will be family prosperity. If four, there will be an increase in the number of children. If five, vain expenses will occur. If six remains, good health will prevail. If seven, poverty will result.

# The Science of Architecture

If the remainder is eight, Goddess Lakshmi <note>The goddess of wealth and prosperity</note> will reside there.
If it is nine, the life of children will depart <note>interpreted as a warning of misfortune for the next generation</note>.
If it is ten, it is considered excellent.
Thus, the Great King has declared and bestowed this knowledge.

**Prose Explanation:** If the result is eight, it will be the abode of Lakshmi. If nine, it results in the loss of children. If ten, it is excellent. (Verse 20).

Regarding the relationship between Income <term>Aya: the mathematical 'gain' or influx of energy</term> and Expenditure <term>Vyaya: the mathematical 'loss' or outflow</term>: If the expenditure exceeds the income, it is harmful. If the income exceeds the expenditure, it is beneficial. If income and expenditure are equal, the results are neutral.

-> **6. Compatibility of the Yoni** <-
<note>Yoni here refers to the 'womb' or 'source' orientation, a calculation used to determine the direction and character of a building.</note>

Multiply the previously mentioned Income by three and divide by eight;
The remainder that stands revealed is the Yoni.
It will manifest its effects like a servant, O Maiden.

**Prose Explanation:** Multiply the previously calculated Income (Aya) by three and divide by eight. The remainder (from 1 to 8) should be known as the Eight Yonis.

The first is said to be auspicious fortune;
The second brings great gold and wealth;
The pure third brings victory;
The fourth is the Dog, which they say indicates disease.

**Prose Explanation:** 
1. If the remainder is **one**, the direction is East (ruled by Indra). This is the **Garuda Yoni** <note>The mythical eagle-king</note>. It brings undiminished fortune forever.
2. If the remainder is **two**, the direction is South-East (ruled by Agni). This is the **Cat Yoni**. It leads to quarrels and conflict.
3. If the remainder is **three**, the direction is South (ruled by Yama). This is the **Lion Yoni**. It brings victory and success.
4. If the remainder is **four**, the direction is South-West (ruled by Nirriti). This is the **Dog Yoni**. It results in constant illness. (Verse 24).

The fifth shown is the Serpent and wealth;
The sixth joined is the treasury of beauty;
The seventh added is the Elephant, a joyful heaven;
The eighth extended is the Crow, which yields no fruit.

**Prose Explanation:**
5. If the remainder is **five**, the direction is West (ruled by Varuna). This is the **Serpent Yoni**. Wealth and riches will multiply.
6. If the remainder is **six**, the direction is North-West (ruled by Vayu). This is the **Rat Yoni**. All possessions will be lost, resulting in misfortune.
7. If the remainder is **seven**, the direction is North (ruled by Kubera). This is the **Elephant Yoni**. That family will live in auspicious prosperity.
8. If the remainder is **eight**, the direction is North-East (ruled by Ishana). This is the **Rabbit Yoni** <note>The text mentions 'Rabbit' in prose but 'Crow' in verse; both are traditionally considered inauspicious in this context</note>. It results in nothingness and lack of fortune.

-> **5. Effects of the Birth Stars** <-

Rohini, Revati, Pushya, Hasta, Mrigashira, Anuradha, Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada—these nine are excellent.

# House-Building Science 31
31

The stars Chitra, Punarvasu, Mula, Dhanishta, Swati, and Shatabhisha—these six are auspicious. Others are of mixed quality.

Ashwini, Bharani, Ardra, Ashlesha, Chitra <note>original: "Chitra" is repeated here in the source list of both good and bad, likely a scribal error or referring to specific quarters of the star</note>, Magha, Purva Phalguni, Vishakha, Jyeshtha, Purva Ashadha, Shravana, and Purva Bhadrapada—these twelve are mediocre and should be avoided.

### 6. Results of the Lunar Days

Regarding this, the waxing phase of the moon <term>Purva-paksha: the bright fortnight</term> is good. The waning phase <term>Apara-paksha: the dark fortnight</term> is mediocre. The eighth lunar day and the new moon day are considered mediocre among the mediocre.

### 7. Suitability of the Days of the Week

->Multiply the house's dimensions by nine<-
->Divide the total by seven and take the remainder<-
->If the remainder corresponds to the Sun, there will be anger<-
->If it falls on the Moon, prosperity will flourish<-

<margin>(31)</margin>
(Meaning) Multiply the previously calculated <term>Aya: the architectural income/dimensions</term> by nine and divide the result by seven. If the remainder corresponds to Sunday <note>the first day</note>, there will always be cruelty and anger in that house. If the remainder is two, corresponding to Monday, the house will flourish day by day with wonderful abundance.

->If it is Mars, wealth will be destroyed<-
->If it is Mercury, there will be joy and comfort<-
->If it is Jupiter, the house will be blessed with children<-
->If it is Venus, goodness is spoken of<-

<margin>(32)</margin>
(Meaning) If the remainder is three, corresponding to Tuesday, old wealth will be lost and no new wealth will come. If the remainder is four, corresponding to Wednesday, the Goddess of Fortune <note>Lakshmi</note> will reside there. If the remainder is five, corresponding to Thursday, there will be the blessing of children. If the remainder is six, corresponding to Friday, all endeavors will result in goodness. If the remainder is seven, corresponding to Saturday, there will be constant quarreling and fear of theft.

### 8. Suitability of the Zodiac Signs

Taurus, Leo, Scorpio, and Aquarius—these four are "Fixed" signs <term>Sthira Rasi</term>; they bring goodness.

Gemini, Virgo, Sagittarius, and Pisces—these four are "Dual" signs <term>Ubaya Rasi</term>. Aries, Cancer, Libra, and Capricorn—these four are "Movable" signs <term>Chara Rasi</term>. These are considered mediocre.

### 9. Suitability of the Castes

Brahmin—neutral; Kshatriya—good; Vaishya—good; Shudra—mediocre. <note>In traditional Vastu Shastra, these categories often referred to the intended use or "character" of the building rather than just the inhabitants.</note>

## 10. The Results of the Portions (Amsha)

1. If the remainder is 1: The portion is bad.
2. If the remainder is 2: The portion of the Moon-maker; brings fortune.
3. If the remainder is 3: The portion of Truth; goodness will occur.
4. If the remainder is 4: The portion of the Lotus-pool; grain and wealth will increase.
5. If the remainder is 5: The portion of Jupiter; there will be the power to rule a kingdom.
6. If the remainder is 6: The portion of Venus; brings the fruit of good fortune.
7. If the remainder is 7: The portion of Saturn; disease will occur, it is bad.
8. If the remainder is 8: The portion of Rahu <note>The North Node of the Moon, associated with eclipses</note>; poverty and great evil.
9. If the remainder is 9: The portion of Ketu <note>The South Node of the Moon</note>; all things will be good.

## 11. The Age of the House

A total sum exceeding 60 years is good. If it is less than 60, that house is unfavorable. Even if the income and expenditure calculations vary, it is best if the income is higher. Even if wisdom increases, if the portion, day, fortnight, womb-type (yoni), week, or zodiac sign are unfavorable, or if the lifespan is short, it is not suitable for a house.

## The Results of the Elements (Bhuta)

Multiply the aforementioned fixed base number by 3 and divide by 5. If the remainder is 1, it is the Earth element; its result is the bestowing of fortune.

> If 2 remains: The Water element; its result is happiness.
> If 3 remains: The Fire element; its result is the fear of death.
> If 4 remains: The Air element; its result is comfort.
> If 5 remains: The Ether element; its result is poverty.

## Determination of the Lineage (Kula)

Multiply the aforementioned fixed base number by 6 and divide by 12.
If 1 remains: Aries. If 2 remains: Taurus. If 3 remains: Gemini. If 4 remains: Cancer. If 5 remains: Leo. If 6 remains: Virgo. If 7 remains: Libra. If 8 remains: Scorpio. If 9 remains: Sagittarius. If 10 remains: Capricorn. If 11 remains: Aquarius. If 12 remains: Pisces.
Whichever number results, that will be the zodiac sign following the four-footed star <note>referring to the Nakshatra or lunar mansion calculation</note>.

<page-num>17</page-num>
<header>CHAPTER II. CONCERNING ST. JOHN THE EVANGELIST</header>

## Chapter II.

### Concerning St. John the Evangelist.

<margin>St. Augustine, Tractate 36 on John.</margin>
Although all the Divine Scriptures proclaim the glory of Christ, St. John the Evangelist performed this more excellently; he is entirely devoted to commending the divinity of the Word and the <term>Consubstantiality</term> <note>The theological doctrine that the Father and the Son are of the exact same substance or essence.</note> of the Father and the Son. And therefore, he is rightly compared by the Church to the eagle, which flies higher than all other birds and gazes upon the sun with steady eyes. This same St. John was distinguished by several singular gifts, setting him apart from the other Evangelists and indeed from almost all the Apostles.

<margin>St. Jerome, Book 1 against Jovinianus.</margin>
First, indeed, because he remained a <term>Virgin</term>, and this by a singular favor of God. For (as St. Jerome writes) when he was about to be married, having been called by Christ, he preserved his <term>Virginity</term> intact until the end of his life. Hence Christ, while hanging on the Cross, entrusted His Virgin Mother to the <term>Virgin</term> disciple.

<margin>John 13 & 21.</margin>
Secondly, on account of that special love with which Christ pursued him, so that he, above the others, was called <term>the disciple whom Jesus loved</term>. A sign of this love was that at the Last Supper he reclined upon the breast of the Lord.

<margin>John 19 & 20.</margin>
Thirdly, because he alone of the Apostles was present at the death of Christ, and because he alone, after the Resurrection, outran Peter to the tomb.

<margin>Tertullian, On Prescription against Heretics, ch. 36.</margin>
<margin>Jerome, On Illustrious Men.</margin>
Fourthly, because having been led to Rome by the <term>Tyrant</term> <note>Referring to the Emperor Domitian, known for his persecution of Christians.</note> Domitian and cast into boiling oil, he emerged unharmed, preserved by divine power for the preaching of the Gospel. Afterward, having been banished to the island of Patmos, he wrote the <term>Apocalypse</term> <note>The Book of Revelation.</note> there. Finally, under the Emperor Trajan, in the hundredth year after the coming of Christ, he most blessedly fell asleep in the Lord at <term>Ephesus</term>.

<sig>B</sig>
<meta>catchword: In-</meta>

<summary>This page details the unique spiritual honors and life events of St. John the Evangelist, emphasizing his theological focus on Christ's divinity and his miraculous survival of martyrdom.</summary>
<keywords>St. John the Evangelist, Consubstantiality, Virginity, Beloved Disciple, Domitian, Patmos, Apocalypse, Ephesus, Trajan</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the narrative of Johan de Witt’s leadership during the mid-17th century, specifically focusing on his diplomatic tensions with France and his internal administration of the Dutch Republic.</meta>

# HISTORY OF THE UNITED PROVINCES.

<margin>His high words to the Ambassadors of *France*.</margin>
D’ESTRADES <note>Godefroi, Comte d'Estrades (1607–1686), a French diplomat and Marshal</note>, who with *de Witt* was then managing a Treaty for the Partition of the *Spanish Netherlands* <note>The region roughly corresponding to modern-day Belgium, then under Spanish rule</note>, and had already proposed the Seizure of *Dunkirk*, *Ostend*, *Nieuport*, and other Sea-ports, to prevent their falling into the hands of the *English*, thought it high time to take notice of the *Dutchman*’s Pride. He represented to him, *That his Master <note>King Louis XIV of France</note> would never suffer the States to give Law to the World; and that he should take care how he despised his Alliance; for his Power was such, that he could make himself either a very good Friend or a very dangerous Enemy.* But *de Witt* was so far from being intimidated, that he told him, *The World was at Peace; but if the King of* France *intended to break it, the States would not be the first to begin: Yet if he should be so unadvis'd as to make a Breach with them, they should know how to defend themselves; and that his Master would find them as good at Arms as they had ever been in Council.*

<margin>He governs the States with an absolute sway.</margin>
IN this lofty manner did he treat the greatest Prince then in *Europe*. He govern’d the States with as absolute an Authority as any Prince in the World did his Subjects. He was sometimes call’d *King of Holland*, and at other times the *grand Pensionary* (for his proper Title was only <term>Pensionary</term> <note>The leading magistrate and legal advisor of the province of Holland, effectively the head of government</note>) *of* Holland. To keep the People in awe, and to curb the <term>Orange</term> <note>The political faction supporting the House of Orange-Nassau, who sought to restore the office of Stadtholder</note> Faction, he maintain’d a powerful Army; and to be at leisure to manage the Affairs of the State at home, he was ever for preserving Peace with his Neighbours. All the while he was at the head of the Republick, he was particularly careful of the <term>Navy</term> <note>The Dutch fleet was the primary instrument of their global trade and defense</note>, which he brought to its greatest Perfection. He gave the Command of the Fleet to <term>de Ruyter</term> <note>Michiel de Ruyter (1607–1676), celebrated as one of the most skilled admirals in history</note>, who, from a Cabbin-boy, was become the greatest Admiral of the Age. He had a profound Knowledge of the Sea, and design’d several Charts and Maps for the use of the <term>Mariners</term> <note>Sailors and navigators</note>. By his indefatigable Industry he had so well improv’d the publick Revenue, that he paid all the Debts of the State, and was able to provide for its Security. He had a great contempt for Riches; and though he lived in the midst of Plenty, he was very frugal in his manner of Life. He walked to the <term>States-General</term>’s <term>Assembly</term> <note>The federal parliament of the United Provinces</note> followed by one Servant; and when he went into the Country, he was attended with only one more. He would not

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the nature of light, specifically applying Cartesian mechanical principles to explain how light travels and interacts with matter.</meta>

# ON THE NATURE OF LIGHT

... might think it has been brought to completion. But to return to the matter at hand: it is clear from this by what reasoning it is demonstrated in <term>Catoptrics</term> <note>the study of reflected light</note> and <term>Dioptrics</term> <note>the study of refracted light, or light passing through lenses</note> that luminous rays can only be carried through lines very close to straight lines. For the rays which geometers consider are nothing other than <term>linear motions</term>, or straight lines, according to which motion and the communication of motion are carried out. However, for those lines to be straight, it is necessary that the <term>globules</term> <note>small spherical particles that Descartes theorized filled all space</note> (whose centers mark those lines) touch each other in close proximity. Furthermore, for them to touch closely, they must be extremely small and crowded together; otherwise, they would not represent a line, but rather some interrupted series of globules which would deviate greatly from a straight line.

---

<margin>Why light is diffused through a shadow.</margin>
From this, the reason is also clear why light is diffused through a shadow, even though it proceeds only through a straight line. For let there be (in <term>figure 53</term>) a luminous point $A$, a <term>transparent</term> medium filled with globules $B$ $C$, and an opaque object $D$ $E$. The light will certainly proceed in a straight line toward $D$ and $E$, but there it will be stopped by the opaque body. Meanwhile, however, the globules which are outside the straight lines $A$ $D$ and $A$ $E$ will not cease to strike those which are at $F$ and $G$; and these in turn strike others at $H$ and $I$; and finally the pulse will reach $L$, although not in a straight line from $A$. Nor does it follow from this that the light must be as strong at $L$ as at $M$: because the entire force of the pulse which is at $A$ comes to point $M$, whereas only some part comes to $L$, since not all the globules from every side strike the one at $L$, as is clear from an inspection of the figure.

---

<margin>The reason for refraction based on motion.</margin>
Since, indeed, in this <term>Cartesian system</term> <note>the philosophical and scientific framework of René Descartes</note> all things must be sought from motion and <term>mechanical</term> reasons, we must see how it happens that rays are broken in <term>refraction</term>. For let there be (in <term>figure 54</term>) a body moved along the line $A$ $B$, and let it strike the surface $C$ $D$ of another medium. It is certain that it cannot continue along the straight line $B$ $E$, but must be bent toward $F$, because the surface $C$ $D$ resists the motion. That resistance, moreover, takes away some part of the velocity; and when a part of the velocity is removed, the body is no longer carried along a straight line, but through a certain <term>curve</term>, the <term>tangent</term> of which is $B$ $F$. But if that body moves faster in the second medium than in the first (as <term>Descartes</term> supposes concerning light), then the ray will be refracted <term>toward the perpendicular</term>, because the force which impels the body in the second medium is greater than the resistance of the surface.

<sig>A a 3</sig>
<meta>catchword: But</meta>

<keywords>optics, René Descartes, refraction, catoptrics, dioptrics, globules, mechanical philosophy, light transmission</keywords>

# On the Elements.

<margin>78</margin>
## I. Fire. <note>original: "Ignis / Das Feuer"</note>

1. *Flame*, (the flame / blaze.)
2. *Spark*, (the spark.)
3. *Smoke*, (the smoke.)
4. *Soot*, (the soot.)
5. *Coal*, (the coal.)
6. *Live coal*, (the glowing coal.)
7. *Ash*, (the ash.)
8. *Hot embers*, (the hot ash.)

---

<margin>79</margin>
## II. Air. <note>original: "Aer / Die Lufft"</note>

1. *Breeze*, (the little air / breeze.)
2. *Wind*, (the wind.)
3. *Whirlwind*, (the whirlwind.)
4. *Storm*, (the violence / the storm.)
5. *Weather*, (the weather / tempest.)
6. *Cloud*, (the cloud.)
7. *Mist*, (the fog or mist.)
8. *Rainbow*, (the rainbow.) <note>Iris was the Greek goddess of the rainbow and a messenger of the gods.</note>

---

<margin>80</margin>
## III. Water. <note>original: "Aqua / Das Waſſer"</note>

1. *Spring*, (the well / the source.)
2. *River*, (the river.)
3. *Torrent*, (the mountain stream.)
4. *Bank*, (the shore / bank.)
5. *Pool*, (the swamp / pond.)
6. *Lake*, (the lake.)
7. *Sea*, (the sea.)
8. *Open sea*, (the high sea / deep ocean.)

---

<margin>81</margin>
## IV. Earth. <note>original: "Terra / Die Erde"</note>

1. *Mountain*, (the mountain.)
2. *Valley*, (the valley.)
3. *Hill*, (the hill.)
4. *Plain*, (the field / the plain.)
5. *Rock*, (the rock.)
6. *Crag*, (the cliff.)
7. *Shore*, (the coast.)
8. *Island*, (the island.)
